14:Tba4,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: N/A - standalone contributor-compute initiative; add to a maintainer-selected board if desired.
- Parent tracker or epic: N/A - this issue is the launch card.
- Coordination Discussion: N/A.
- Suggested lane tag: eliza-computer-codex

## Scope

Launch `eliza.computer` from this monorepo as a minimal Vite site that turns spare agent compute into reviewable elizaOS contributions. Ship a downloadable/runnable skill that selects and finishes issues, reviews open PRs, adds missing tests/evidence/validation/screenshots/video, follows the repository claim and evidence workflow, and discloses the exact model used. Add a transparent GitHub-backed contribution leaderboard, Cloudflare Pages CI/CD, and the contribution/template changes needed to make model attribution durable.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A bundled, validated, installable contribution skill handles issue-finishing and PR-review workflows safely.
- [ ] The skill enforces claims, sync/rebase, package guidance, scoped validation, real evidence, independent review, and model disclosure.
- [ ] A responsive, accessible Vite site explains the workflow, installs/downloads the skill, surfaces open work, and renders a transparent elizaOS/eliza leaderboard.
- [ ] Leaderboard scoring covers merged PRs, issue work, substantive comments/reviews, evidence, and model disclosure with anti-gaming caps and documented methodology.
- [ ] PR/issue contribution guidance records model provenance, including a human-only declaration path.
- [ ] Unit, integration, build, accessibility, responsive, and real browser E2E checks pass; desktop/mobile screenshots and an MP4 walkthrough are manually reviewed.
- [ ] Cloudflare Pages deployment and custom domain are configured and production is verified over HTTPS.
- [ ] CI/CD refreshes leaderboard data and deploys only validated builds.

## Evidence Plan

Keep every applicable row visible. Use `N/A - <reason>` only when a row truly does not apply.

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: before/current reference plus after desktop/mobile JPGs and full-flow MP4.
- [ ] Logs: browser console/network, build/test logs, GitHub data refresh, and Cloudflare deploy/HTTP verification.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: forward-test the skill on real repository work and attach the model-named trajectory/output.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: packaged skill archive, generated leaderboard JSON, live Pages deployment, DNS/TLS response.
- [ ] CI or local commands: package tests/typecheck/lint/build/E2E plus repository verify after rebase.

## Claim / Status Notes

Claim will be posted before implementation. Production deploy and DNS are shared levers and will be claimed explicitly before mutation.

## Model disclosure

- Model(s) used: OpenAI GPT-5 (Codex; exact serving snapshot is not exposed to this session).
- Agent/tooling: Codex desktop agent with repository, GitHub, browser, and Cloudflare tooling.
- Human-only work: none at issue creation.15:Tadc,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: Launch QA (Project 12)
- Related prior work: #17379 / merged PR #17380 covers provider-neutral exhausted-credit copy only; it does not repair provider selection or capability ownership.
- Suggested lane tag: `codex-direct-provider-ownership`

## Problem

A runtime can use Eliza Cloud for managed capabilities while the owner routes text or embeddings directly to Cerebras, Ollama, OpenAI, or local inference. Cloud-mode plugin collection currently strips direct model providers broadly, and Cloud environment hydration can return early when the canonical `serviceRouting` topology exists without the deprecated `config.cloud` block. The persisted route can therefore name a provider that the runtime never loads, or Cloud inference can displace the owner's direct route.

## Scope

Only the agent boot/provider arbitration boundary:

- `packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts`
- `packages/agent/src/runtime/plugin-collector.ts`
- their two focused policy test files

No UI, core reply copy, Cloud backend, deployment, migration, device, or credential-store changes.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A Cloud runtime with a direct `llmText` route retains the provider plugin that owns that route while still loading Cloud-managed capabilities.
- [ ] Direct capability routes retain only their explicitly routed local/direct provider plugins; unrelated local model surfaces stay removed.
- [ ] Canonical routes work without the deprecated `config.cloud` block and preserve/hydrate Cloud proxy credentials through existing supported environment/config paths.
- [ ] Cerebras resolves through the existing OpenAI-compatible provider mapping; Ollama, OpenAI, and local-inference ownership are covered.
- [ ] Cloud-proxy-only and remote/local runtime semantics remain unchanged.
- [ ] Focused provider/config tests, agent typecheck, scoped Biome, diff/error-policy checks pass.
- [ ] Supported server-side live-provider evidence is captured without reading, printing, or exporting credentials; if unavailable, the PR remains draft with the exact gap.

## Evidence plan

- Runtime/plugin-policy test output and exact patch-equivalence proof.
- Supported local server health/config/provider result and one live Cerebras reply only if the route is already configured and authorized; secrets must remain opaque.
- Screenshots/video: N/A unless a user-visible flow is exercised; this change touches no rendered source.
- Real-LLM trajectory: attach only if the supported route succeeds; otherwise explicitly document why the PR is draft.
- Domain artifacts: N/A - provider selection does not write domain state.

## Claim

CLAIMED by `codex-direct-provider-ownership`. No merge, deployment, Cloud mutation, or device action is authorized by this issue.

17:Tf6d,## Summary

The Light Phone 3 (LightOS, 1080x1240 portrait, small panel) chat/home UI regressed badly between the Jul 23 demo-day build and current `develop`. On `22ec1c51` the home surface is unusable enough that we rolled the physical demo device back to `d2dfd159`.

Both builds were installed on the **same physical device** (`LP3LHMA632300536`), same debug signature, upgrade-in-place, so this is a pure client-UI delta, not a device/config difference.

- **Good:** `d2dfd159` (Jul 23, demo-day build)
- **Broken:** `22ec1c51` (`fix(ci): generate i18n outputs before filtered builds (#17483)`)

## Symptoms

### 1. Notifications collapsed into a tiny strip

`uiautomator` bounds for the `Notifications` region, keyboard closed, identical device/orientation (1080x1240):

| build | Notifications bounds | height |
|---|---|---|
| `d2dfd159` (good) | `[48,408][1032,1086]` | **678px** |
| `22ec1c51` (broken) | `[24,408][1056,774]` | **366px** |

The notification region loses ~46% of its height. On a panel this short that leaves roughly two cards visible with everything else behind a "Show all 41 notifications" button.

### 2. App launcher row pushed off the home surface

On `d2dfd159` the launcher is laid out and hit-testable:

```
View   | Apps       | [48,1122][1032,1240]
Button | Settings   | [75,1146][267,1240]
Button | Wallet     | [321,1146][513,1240]
Button | Projects   | [567,1146][759,1240]
Button | Automations| [789,1146][1029,1240]
```

On `22ec1c51` those same nodes report **`[0,0][0,0]`** (present in the tree, zero-area, not hittable), and a `Home content` block `[48,810][1032,987]` appears in their place.

### 3. Keyboard/composer spacing

With the IME open (`mInputShown=true`) the composer occupies the same 537px on both builds, but the conversation-history viewport differs (`381px` broken vs `363px` good) while the usable chat area above it is materially smaller on `22ec1c51` because of the home-surface reflow above. Reported by the device owner as "keyboard covers more than half the page and doesn't resize content", plus large gaps between bubbles and under the reply box that force scrolling to reach content that used to fit.

## Suspect

**PR #17151 — "fix: integrate reviewed LP3 demo UI and Simple Views stack"**, specifically commit `282c82c5a4f` *"fix(ui): restore responsive home pager"* (Jul 23 21:32 EDT).

That commit is **in `22ec1c51` and NOT in `d2dfd159`** (verified with `git merge-base --is-ancestor`), and it introduces:

```css
[data-home-notification-region] {
  flex-grow: 0;
  max-height: 40%;
  ...
}
```

A hard `max-height: 40%` on the notification region is consistent with symptom 1. The same commit moves the launcher off the home surface ("launcher apps remain on the adjacent swipe page", `LauncherSurface` import removed from `HomeScreen.tsx`), which is consistent with symptom 2 — the zero-area app buttons.

Caveat, stated plainly: I have **not** rebuilt with that commit reverted, so this is a strong evidence-backed suspect, not a confirmed bisect. Symptom 3's exact cause I would call **unknown** — the range also contains several chat-layout commits (`c5eab587650` reserve chat overlay clearance, `c30679cb01d` preserve chat action footer geometry, `f76f3d7f031` native chat glass) that I did not isolate.

## Why this matters for LP3

The percentage-based `max-height: 40%` and the assumption that the launcher can live on a separate swipe page both behave acceptably on a normal tall phone viewport and degrade sharply at 1240px. LP3 is the short-panel case that catches it.

## Environment

- Device: Light Phone 3 (`TLP301`), LightOS, serial `LP3LHMA632300536`
- Display: 1080x1240, density 480, portrait
- Package: `ai.elizaos.app`, debug build, targetSdk 35
- IME: `com.android.inputmethod.latin/.LatinIME`

## Artifacts

`uiautomator` XML dumps and screencaps for both builds, keyboard open and closed, were captured and are attached/available on request.
18:T1128,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: N/A - standalone CI-gate defect surfaced during independent review of #17496 / #17526 / #17529 / #17539.
- Parent tracker or epic: N/A.
- Coordination Discussion: N/A.
- Suggested lane tag: claude-code-ss251

## Scope

Plugin unit tests do not run **pre-merge** for pull requests targeting `develop`, so a defect that only a plugin spec can catch lands on `develop` and is discovered post-merge.

Verified against `origin/develop`:

- `.github/workflows/test.yml` — has no `pull_request` trigger at all (`push: [develop]`, `workflow_dispatch`, `schedule`). Its sharded `plugin-tests` job (`bun run test:plugins`, `TEST_PACKAGE_FILTER='\(plugins/'`) therefore only runs **after** merge.
- `.github/workflows/windows-ci.yml` — its `plugins` lane does run `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-app-control test`, and its path filter includes `plugins/plugin-app-control/**`, but its `pull_request` trigger is scoped to `branches: [main]`. For `develop` it fires only on `push`, i.e. post-merge. (#17529 admits develop-targeted PRs to this workflow; that closes part of the gap and is the reason this issue is scoped to the develop PR gate rather than to that trigger.)
- `.github/workflows/develop-pr.yml` — lint, format, typecheck, build. No unit tests.

This is not a coverage hole: `app.test.ts` **is** covered post-merge on both Linux and the Windows plugins lane. It is a *timing* hole, and it is load-bearing.

**Concrete cost, from this week:** commit `e59dbd8dea` ("enforce the single-voice contract across the action surface, 149 sites") rewrote the owner-denial copy in `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/app.ts` and updated seven sibling specs but missed `app.test.ts:43`. Nothing pre-merge could catch it, so it merged red and stayed red on `develop`. It then surfaced only as collateral — reported inside #17495/#17496 as a "Windows" failure, which it is not (it is platform-independent; develop push run 30691583093 fails identically on Linux) — and consumed a separate follow-up PR (#17525, closed as a duplicate of #17539) plus a red required check on the unrelated #17529.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A develop-targeted pull request that breaks a plugin unit test fails a **required** check before merge.
- [ ] The gate runs the same runner CI already uses for these packages (`vitest run` via each package's `test` script), not a different runner.
- [ ] Reverting `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/app.test.ts:43` to the pre-#17539 assertion makes the new gate go red, and restoring it makes it green — demonstrated, not asserted.
- [ ] Added wall-clock to the develop PR gate is stated and bounded; if the full plugin sweep is too slow for the gate, a changed-path-scoped subset is acceptable provided the previous criterion still holds for a changed plugin.
- [ ] No change to post-merge coverage in `test.yml`, and no duplication of the Windows lane's responsibilities.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A - CI configuration change with no rendered surface.
- [ ] Logs: the deliberate-break CI run (red) and the restored run (green) on the same PR head, linked.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, action, or provider behavior changes.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: N/A - the CI run records are the artifact.
- [ ] CI or local commands: `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-app-control test`, plus the workflow run URLs for the red/green pair.

## Claim / Status Notes

Filed from independent review, not claimed. I have no objection to the fleet taking it; if it is still unclaimed I may claim it later under the lane tag above. Whoever takes it should coordinate with #17529, which is already editing `windows-ci.yml`'s trigger — this issue is deliberately scoped to the **develop PR gate** to avoid conflicting with that change.

- AI assistance: yes
- AI provider/model: Anthropic / claude-fable-5

- Attribution status: self-reported

19:T5a4,## Problem

The self-hosted runner workspace pruner treats every direct child of `_work` as a disposable repository checkout. GitHub Actions also owns persistent control directories there, including `_temp`, `_actions`, `_tool`, `_update`, and `_PipelineMapping`.

This can delete `_work/_temp/_runner_file_commands/save_state_*` while an action is running, causing otherwise unrelated jobs to fail after checkout. The exact signature occurred in workflow runs 30692175805 and 30691649390 on `eliza-robot-6`; the same revisions passed when scheduled on other runners.

## Acceptance criteria

- The prune plan never selects GitHub Actions runner-owned control directories.
- Stale ordinary repository workspaces remain eligible for pruning.
- A regression test covers every protected control directory and proves the test fails if the protection is removed.
- Existing parser, discovery, and stale/fresh behavior remains green.

## Evidence

- Backend logs: workflow logs in runs 30692175805 and 30691649390 show `save_state_*` disappearing immediately after checkout.
- Frontend logs: N/A - runner administration only.
- Screenshots/video: N/A - no user-facing UI.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no agent/model behavior.
- Domain artifact: prune-plan regression test and mutation proof.
- Native/platform capture: N/A - Linux runner filesystem behavior.
- Manual review: repository pruner behavior and public runner directory contract inspected.1a:T5ba,## Problem

A single physical Cerebras `gemma-4-31b` request is recorded twice in the database trajectory aggregate: once by the provider-level wire recorder and again by the generic `runtime.useModel()` fallback recorder.

Live evidence from #17531:

- native trajectory: one model stage, 4,142 prompt tokens, 52 completion tokens;
- DB aggregate: `llmCallCount = 2`, 8,284 prompt tokens, 104 completion tokens;
- the values are exactly doubled, while wire/native evidence proves only one network request.

This corrupts request counts, token totals, cost, and latency attribution. Removing either recorder globally is unsafe: providers with detailed wire telemetry should win, while model handlers without it still need the generic fallback.

## Acceptance criteria

- Introduce request-local recording scope around each `runtime.useModel()` call.
- When a nested provider/wire recorder records the call, suppress only that call's generic fallback record.
- When no nested recorder runs, preserve the generic runtime record.
- Concurrent `useModel()` calls cannot suppress one another.
- Record attempts that are rejected/not accepted do not suppress the generic fallback.
- Add deterministic unit coverage for provider-recorded, generic-only, rejected-record, error, and concurrent mixed cases.
- Re-run a live Cerebras benchmark and prove one physical request produces `llmCallCount = 1` with non-doubled tokens in both native and DB telemetry.

Related: #17531
1b:T577,## Problem

The benchmark CLI races `handleMessage()` against a fixed 120-second timer, but timing out does not abort the underlying message turn. It then calls `process.exit()` on every terminal path without stopping the runtime or closing the database adapter. Live Cerebras evidence from PR #17519 consequently left the trajectory row `active` after the process exited.

This creates three false signals:

- a benchmark can report timeout while the runtime keeps generating and writing;
- abrupt exit can kill tracked post-delivery work before trajectory finalization;
- the caller cannot distinguish a cleanly completed run from an abandoned database row.

## Acceptance criteria

- Remove the benchmark task timeout option and its fixed timer race from both CLI entry points.
- Give each benchmark process/server an owner `AbortSignal` and pass it into the real message service path.
- SIGINT/SIGTERM abort active work and then run normal runtime/database shutdown; no arbitrary grace timeout.
- Every post-boot return path executes `shutdownRuntime()` and avoids `process.exit()`.
- Server EOF and one-shot completion drain tracked post-delivery work before adapter close.
- Cover completion, cancellation, malformed input, and cleanup with focused tests.
- Run a live Cerebras `gemma-4-31b` task and inspect persisted trajectory state to prove it is terminal after CLI exit.

Related: #17519

```typescript
import { call, getOrCreateWallet, listServices } from 'minia2a-client';
```

1f:T125f,## Problem

On a fork PR from a first-time contributor, GitHub holds every
`pull_request`-triggered workflow at `action_required` until a maintainer
approves the run. Both aggregate gates trigger on `pull_request_target`, which is
exempt from that approval, so they start immediately and poll for checks that
cannot appear. After the full timeout budget they convert "never appeared" into
`FAILED`.

The result is a PR whose required checks are all red without any of them having
evaluated the diff, and roughly 20-40 minutes of runner time consumed per gate,
per PR, producing no signal.

## Evidence

Two PRs, different sizes, different subsystems, same failure:

| PR | Diff | Develop PR Gate | Security Advisory Gate |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| #17545 | +1206/-24, 17 files, `packages/cloud/shared` | FAILED | FAILED |
| #17546 | +356/-39, 2 files, Discord onboarding | FAILED | passed |

**Develop PR Gate** — [run 30701753824](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30701753824),
81 polls over ~40 minutes:

```
poll 81: passed=0 waiting=0 failed=7
FAILED lint:               no GitHub Actions check run from .github/workflows/develop-pr.yml
FAILED typecheck:          no GitHub Actions check run from .github/workflows/develop-pr.yml
FAILED build:              no GitHub Actions check run from .github/workflows/develop-pr.yml
FAILED gitleaks:           no GitHub Actions check run from .github/workflows/gitleaks.yml
FAILED check-pr-evidence:  no GitHub Actions check run from .github/workflows/pr.yaml
FAILED check-pr-title:     no GitHub Actions check run from .github/workflows/pr.yaml
FAILED stale-base guard:   no GitHub Actions check run from .github/workflows/stale-base-guard.yml
```

**Security Advisory Gate** — [run 30701716047](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30701716047),
~40 polls over 20 minutes:

```
waiting for deterministic security checks: gitleaks
...
scripts/security/security-advisory-gate.mjs:148
Error: timed out waiting for security advisory checks
```

Workflow run states on the same branch at that moment — none had started:

```
gitleaks         | completed | action_required | pull_request
Cloud Tests      | completed | action_required | pull_request
Develop PR       | completed | action_required | pull_request
Security Review  | completed | action_required | pull_request
Quality (Fork)   | completed | action_required | pull_request
… 13 workflows total
```

The Security Advisory Gate difference between the two PRs is scope, not health:
it did not require `gitleaks` for the smaller diff.

## Why the existing approval handling does not cover this

`security-advisory-gate.mjs` already models approval latency — `approvalDeadline`
plus `requiredChecksStartedBy()` grants a bounded completion grace when required
checks *started* before the deadline. That handles "approved, but slow".

It does not distinguish that from "held at `action_required`, so it will never
start". In the second case the wait cannot succeed by construction, but the gate
still consumes its whole budget before reporting a failure whose message
("timed out") does not name the actual cause.

## Suggested direction

Treat a required check whose workflow run is `action_required` as *not yet
approved* rather than *pending*:

- fail fast with an explicit message ("required workflows awaiting maintainer
  approval: …") instead of polling to exhaustion, or
- skip the gate for fork PRs until approval lands, letting it run on the
  post-approval re-run.

Either turns a silent 20-40 minute timeout into an immediate, actionable
statement, and stops burning runner capacity on a wait that cannot resolve. It
also stops a reviewer seeing seven red required checks that never ran.

Both gates share the shape, so whichever fix applies probably wants to apply to
both.

## Notes

- This is reported from the outside, based on two runs; if the current behaviour
  is deliberate — a gate cannot know a check will never arrive — feel free to
  close.
- I have not claimed this. Happy to pick it up if useful, though I cannot
  reproduce the `action_required` state on demand to verify a fix, so someone
  with repo-side visibility may be better placed.

## Scope

**`https://eliza.app/profile/edit` renders a blank page. No such route exists in the deployed application, so no contributor can currently set a payout address.**

This is the single entry point to the rewards program on both surfaces that advertise it:

- `packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza/SKILL.md` (revision `e37924817dd457f3c5cc1547531c7054336a3740`, "Contributor rewards"): *"To receive USDC, use https://eliza.app/profile/edit to generate a hidden GitHub README comment…"*
- `eliza.army` links it twice — the primary nav (`Payout address`) and the hero CTA (`Set payout address`), directly above the copy *"Add a public Solana or Ethereum address to receive USDC."*

### Evidence

`GET https://eliza.app/profile/edit` returns **HTTP 200** with the standard SPA shell, so this is not a server 404 — the client router simply has no match, and the app renders nothing.

Measured in a real browser at that URL: `document.getElementById("root").children.length === 0` and `innerHTML.length === 0` (the page paints only the brand background). For comparison, `https://eliza.app/` mounts normally with `children.length === 1`.

The route table in the deployed main bundle (`/assets/index-Do7Hzdi9.js`) contains exactly six paths:

```
/            /connected      /downloads
/get-started /leaderboard    /login
```

I also checked the lazily-loaded chunks referenced by that bundle's `__vite__mapDeps` — `authed-shell-BJWuvhmJ.js`, `login-DCuyaPB_.js`, `connected-kNedbuN1.js` — in case the editor were behind an auth boundary. None declares a `/profile` route; the only `profile` hit in `connected` is the asset `/eliza-app-profile-image.webp`. So this is not "log in first": the path is absent from the shipped router, and no session state can make it resolve.

### Impact

Every contributor who follows the skill's own instructions hits a dead end at the last step, after doing the work. The rewards program is otherwise live and the leaderboard is public, so the gap is specifically between "contribution accepted" and "able to be paid."

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] `https://eliza.app/profile/edit` renders the payout-address editor for a signed-in user, or redirects to `/login` and returns there after authentication.
- [ ] An unauthenticated visit produces a visible state (login prompt or redirect) rather than an empty document.
- [ ] The editor accepts a public Solana or Ethereum address, validates its format, and produces the hidden GitHub README comment described in the skill.
- [ ] If the route is intentionally not shipped yet, the two `eliza.army` links and the skill's "Contributor rewards" section point somewhere that works, or state that payout setup is not yet open — so contributors are not sent to a blank page.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds the bundled `contribute-to-eliza` skill for finishing issues and
  reviewing PRs with claims, tests, evidence, validation, and exact model
  disclosure.
- Adds `packages/eliza-computer`, the Vite site for `eliza.army`, with a GitHub
  snapshot, contribution leaderboard, work queue, verified skill download, and
  public payout-address instructions.
- States the offer precisely: a $10,000 monthly USDC pool for accepted elizaOS
  contributions. Leaderboard points do not determine payouts.
- Adds attribution and evidence checks for PRs, issue comments, reviews, and
  repository AI workflows.
- Adds exact-artifact Cloudflare Pages CI/CD with a protected production
  environment.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature, with supporting policy, security, documentation, CI, and tests.

# Documentation

Updated `CONTRIBUTING.md`, issue and PR templates, workflow documentation,
package product/design/deployment guides, the package map, and the skill's
canonical references.

27:T143f,# Relates to

Implements the disabled-by-default Cloud server portion of #16420. The native iOS shell is tracked in #16640; the renderer consumer and live staging walkthrough remain separate integration slices.

# Summary

- Add a first-party Authorization Code + PKCE lifecycle for the fixed `ai.elizaos.app` client and exact `https://eliza.app/auth/callback` redirect.
- Mint a short-lived hash-only authorization code, create one inactive encrypted credential on exchange, and activate it transactionally only after the native client proves durable Keychain receipt.
- Add crash/retry-safe exchange, acknowledgement, self-revocation, signed-in account recovery, expiry cleanup, app-deactivation revocation, and additive database migrations.
- Keep mobile credentials primary-consistent and outside positive auth caches; prevent them from managing generic credentials or delegated access.
- Apply route-isolated admission/ingress limits and reject mobile credentials in WebSocket query strings. Ordinary Cloud/browser WebSocket tokens retain their existing behavior.
- Regenerate the canonical Hono router and public SDK wrappers.

# Rollout and compatibility

- The feature fails closed unless `ELIZA_MOBILE_APP_AUTH_ENABLED` is exactly `true`, the environment is exactly `staging` or `production`, and `ELIZA_MOBILE_APP_AUTH_APP_ID` is a live approved app UUID whose allowed origins include the fixed callback.
- Neither flag is configured by this PR. No staging or production deployment/enablement was performed from this branch.
- Migrations are additive. Existing API keys, sessions, browser auth, Android, desktop, local, and remote-host flows retain their existing contracts.
- Mobile credentials use headers only. The server returns `mobile_credential_transport_forbidden` for `eliza_mobile_*` URL query tokens so a long-lived secret is never forwarded through the generic WebSocket channel.
- The credential is finite-lived (30 days), attributable to the configured app, individually recoverable/revocable, and tombstoned with encrypted reveal material erased.

2b:T49e2,# Relates to

Rework of #17462 against @lalalune's CHANGES_REQUESTED review of `7a17e01969572aa61b7651cd45deb1926fa530f1`. Fix-forward on the same branch. Every blocker is addressed at the root, not patched around.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto the latest `origin/develop` with zero conflicts.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `ba3f094640a` (exact current `origin/develop` at push time); zero conflicts. Develop moved repeatedly during this work (`dc873211...`, `bb9cc985...`, now `ba3f0946...`); this is recut onto the latest.
- [x] Package-scoped verification green post-rebase. `bun run verify` (full monorepo) not run to completion — unrelated cost; the affected packages are verified individually below.

# Risks

**Medium.** This is an authorization boundary, so both directions matter.

- **Fail-closed risk (mitigated):** the schema documents `groupPolicy: "allowlist"` as the default. Applying that literally would silently mute every existing env-only deployment that never wrote a policy. Resolution: `allowlist` applies as soon as *any* channel config exists; a deployment with no channel config at all resolves to `open`, and the resolution source is logged (`groupPolicySource: implicit-open|implicit-allowlist|explicit`). Same reasoning for DMs: no `dm` block at all means `legacy` (historical always-allowed).
- **New startup failures (intended):** ambiguous/unresolvable/deactivated identifiers and unhonorable config now throw during `startAccount`. This is the point of the change — an operator is entitled to have their policy enforced or to be told loudly that it is not — but it does mean a config that previously "worked" (by being ignored) can now fail to boot. Every message names the offending key and the fix.
- **`dm.policy="pairing"` now fails closed.** It is the schema default *once a `dm` block exists*, and this connector implements no pairing handshake. Admitting every DM instead would be the silent-acceptance bug again. Logged loudly at startup with the two working alternatives.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`SlackConfigSchema` has long accepted a rich policy surface — per-channel `enabled`/`requireMention`/`users`/`allowBots`, account `groupPolicy`, and a `dm` block — while `SlackService` gated purely on the global `SLACK_SHOULD_RESPOND_ONLY_TO_MENTIONS` env flag and the `SLACK_CHANNEL_IDS` allowlist. **Config that looked applied was silently dropped**, which is the worst possible failure mode for an authorization boundary.

The review established the defect is deeper than the gate helper, so this replaces the helper with one authoritative policy model.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (security-relevant: an authorization boundary that did not exist).

# Blocker-by-blocker response

### [P0-1] Config never reaches the gate — `c764af80f04`

Two independent drops, both fixed:

1. `buildCharacterFromConfig()` projected only the three Slack token env fields, so `connectors.slack` never landed on `character.settings.slack`. Now the non-credential **policy** object is projected as a plain setting (credentials keep flowing through `secrets`, redactable). Agent-entry `settings.slack` still wins so injected sandbox characters override file config.
2. `getMultiAccountConfig()` (accounts.ts) hand-picked 4 keys and discarded the rest. `SlackConfigSchema` is literally `SlackAccountSchema.extend({accounts})` — **the top level IS an account config** — so the object is now passed through whole, and `SlackMultiAccountConfig extends SlackAccountConfig` to match the schema's real shape.

Your exact reproduction is now a test. Controls assert the projection, the account resolution, **and** the enforcement at the registered Bolt handler, from a persisted `ElizaConfig`.

### [P0-2] Structured allowlists fail open — `1e27ba37ec6`

Root fix: **names are resolved to immutable IDs at startup**, once, before any handler is registered. Event-time authorization is an exact opaque-string comparison that never depends on a warm cache. Consequently:

- name-only records no longer produce an empty (therefore allow-all) allowlist;
- a name-keyed `enabled:false` is enforced on the **first** event;
- ambiguous names and unresolvable **restricted** entries **fail startup**;
- `handleMemberJoinedChannel` now **obeys** `groupPolicy` — a join no longer unions into admission, so invite-the-bot is no longer an allowlist bypass;
- `groupPolicy` is enforced (see Risks for the legacy-deployment carve-out), and `allowlist` with an empty allowlist fails startup rather than admitting nothing.

A pure-ID config costs **zero** startup API calls (asserted).

### [P1-3] User allowlist contract — `1e27ba37ec6`

Handlers compare the sender's opaque ID against IDs resolved at startup, so configured handles finally match. Ambiguity, unresolvable entries, and **deactivated** users all fail startup (a freed handle can be reused by another account — that is a live privilege-transfer bug). Per your Slack link, ID prefix shape is used **only** to decide whether a config key is a literal identifier or a name needing resolution; every authorization comparison is exact-match against an API-returned ID.

**`users: []` is now an EXPLICIT empty allowlist that denies everyone** — documented, warned at startup, and tested. Treating it as "no policy" was a fail-open.

### [P1-4] Event classification — `1e27ba37ec6`

`classifySlackEvent()` explicitly separates `im` / `app_home` / `mpim` / `channel` / private-channel. App Home traffic arrives on `D` channel IDs and was matching `channels["*"]`, so a channel-scoped `enabled:false` silently killed DMs. MPIM now runs on `dm.groupEnabled`/`dm.groupChannels` instead of channel policy. Unclassifiable events fail closed.

**DM policy is implemented here** (folding in the old slice 2): `open`/`allowlist`/`disabled` enforced; `pairing` fails closed with a loud startup warning, documented in Risks.

### [P1-5] One authoritative typed policy resolver — `1e27ba37ec6`

`allowlist.ts` is deleted; `policy.ts` owns account/channel/DM/event context in one resolver, reached from both handlers through a single call site so the two paths cannot drift.

`SlackChannelConfig` now mirrors `SlackChannelSchema` **exactly**: `tools`/`toolsBySender` added, invented `replyToMode` removed (the `.strict()` schema never accepted it, so it could not arrive through the production path), `allowBots` consumed. **Config the service cannot honor now FAILS configuration**: per-channel `tools`/`toolsBySender` and `mode:"http"` throw at startup; inert-but-harmless keys raise startup warnings naming exactly what has no effect.

### [P1-6] Evidence — partially complete, **live Slack round trip still needs a human**

Fully done, and the fake-Bolt criticism is addressed: **no test hand-constructs `ResolvedSlackAccount` any more.** `test-harness.ts` deliberately offers no way to inject one, because that shortcut is precisely how a gate receiving no configuration passed its own suite. Every gating test now drives the real chain: persisted `ElizaConfig` to real `buildCharacterFromConfig()` to `character.settings.slack` to real `resolveSlackAccount()` to real `resolveSlackAccountPolicy()` to real `registerEventHandlers()` to the captured Bolt callbacks.

**Not done, and I am not going to claim otherwise:** a real/sandbox Slack round trip with raw inbound payloads, outbound API responses, and reviewed logs. I cannot create a Slack workspace or issue `xoxb-`/`xapp-` tokens from this environment. Exactly what a human needs to do is listed under **Known gaps** below.

### [P0-3] Credentials crossed the secret boundary — `0dca2afeb39`

Your exact-head blocker, and you were right. The previous head assigned the whole canonical `connectors.slack` object to `character.settings.slack`. `SlackAccountSchema` declares `botToken` / `appToken` / `userToken` / `signingSecret` at the base level **and inside every `accounts.<id>` entry**, so all of them left the `settings.secrets` boundary the redactor scans and became ordinary settings — and the assignment shared the persisted object by reference.

Root fix rather than a strip-list:

- `projectConnectorPolicy()` returns a typed credential-free **view**: a deep clone (no shared structure with the persisted config) with every credential-classified key removed **at any depth**. Classification reuses the repo's own `isSensitiveConfigKey`, the same predicate driving config-API redaction, so "what is a credential" has one definition. It is deny-by-predicate, so a credential field added to the schema later is excluded by default instead of leaking.
- The stripped credentials are not lost. Base-level credentials (which every account inherits, since `SlackConfigSchema` is `SlackAccountSchema.extend({accounts})`) and per-account credentials are hoisted to deterministic keys on the **secret** path. Per-account credentials previously had no secret lane at all — that is what made plain-settings projection look necessary for multi-account.
- Key derivation lives once in `@elizaos/core` (`connectorAccountCredentialSettingKey` / `connectorBaseCredentialSettingKey`) so the projecting side and the consuming plugin cannot drift. One key per credential deliberately: the redactor's known-secret set is built from secret *values*, so a single packed JSON blob would only redact as a whole and let individual tokens through.
- `resolveSlackAccount()` reads credentials from the secret path, per-account overriding base, with the flat `SLACK_*` env lane still last and still default-account-only.

Exact-schema tests enumerate the live `SlackAccountSchema` / `SlackConfigSchema` shapes rather than a hand-copied list, and assert base and per-account credential fields never appear in plain character settings (**by key and by value**) while every policy field still reaches the Slack resolver. The independent credential derivation is type-aware, so `userTokenReadOnly` — a boolean policy flag that matches a naive `/token/` name test — is correctly not treated as a credential.

### Typecheck claim correction

You were right. The "107 baseline + 2 new errors" figure in the old body was an unbuilt-core artifact, not a real result. With core built first (`bun run --cwd packages/core build`), `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-slack typecheck` **passes with zero errors**, and I fixed the two genuine type defects this rework introduced rather than reporting around them.

# Documentation changes needed?

No repo docs change. Behaviour that operators must know about is surfaced **at runtime**, which is more reliable than a doc: startup logs the resolved `groupPolicy` + its source, and warns by name for every configured key that has no effect.

2c:Tc2b,## Summary

Progresses #15783 by replacing process-local backup scheduling assumptions with a durable, generation-fenced database lane.

- reserves due backups with database time and `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`
- fences attempts to the exact job claim and sandbox image generation
- prevents a late/reclaimed worker from marking a newer image healthy
- records absent, stale, unsupported, unreachable, repeated-failure, and backlog pressure states distinctly
- deduplicates fleet incidents and verifies non-2xx alert delivery
- caps alert claims to the visible actionable batch so truncated rows remain alertable
- makes snapshot execution fail closed behind `ELIZA_SNAPSHOT_JOBS_ENABLED`

## Why this is not a timer-only patch

The failure mode crosses scheduler, queue, image lifecycle, worker recovery, and operator health. This PR gives those boundaries durable identities and compare-and-swap transitions. A backup only advances freshness when the executing job still owns the exact attempt and image generation it started against; clock skew and stale worker completion cannot fabricate health.

# Risks

**Medium.** This changes inbound admission for every Slack event, so a bug here
would show up as dropped messages rather than a crash. Mitigations built in:

- Every guard fails **open** on missing data. No `event_id` → admitted. No
  `team_id` in the envelope → admitted. Sender-less event → admitted unkeyed
  (never deduped against anything). The only hard drops are on data that is
  present and provably wrong.
- The `message`/`app_mention` twin now yields **only after the winner actually
  dispatched**. Every other outcome (gated, no memory, thrown) releases the lane,
  so the failure direction is "an extra reply" rather than the current silent
  drop. A handler throw frees the lane entirely so Slack's own redelivery still
  works as a retry.
- New logic is in three standalone modules with no imports into existing files;
  `service.ts` changes are wiring only.
- Behaviour with no coordinator present (bare construction) falls back to the
  historical exclusive-path return, so partial construction cannot double-post.

Blast radius is `plugins/plugin-slack` only. No workflow, config-schema, or
shared-package changes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Slack's Events API is an at-least-once transport delivered over two overlapping
streams. The connector treated it as neither. Four correctness gaps, all fixed on
the **real registered Bolt handlers**:

### 1. Event dedupe + debounce keying (`inbound-reliability.ts`)

There was no dedupe cache at all, so a redelivered event ran the agent a second
time. Slack retries whenever the ack is slow, which means **the slower the agent
turn, the more duplicate replies** — exactly backwards from what you want.

Adds a two-level TTL cache: envelope `event_id` (the identity Slack holds
constant across retries, covering families with no channel/ts such as reactions
and member joins) plus a conversation-lane key. Lane keying follows the Slack
conversation model rather than the raw event:

| Case | Key shape | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Thread reply | `channel:thread_ts` | a thread is one lane |
| Reply, `thread_ts` unresolved | `channel:maybe-thread:ts` | must not collide with the channel's top-level lane |
| Top-level channel message | `channel:ts` | otherwise two unrelated messages in a busy channel share a lane and the second is mistaken for a duplicate |
| Top-level DM | `channel` | keeps consecutive short DM lines batchable |
| Sender | `user ?? bot_id` | bot messages carry no `user` |

`retry_num` / `retryNum` / `retry_attempt` are read from all three surfaces Slack
uses. Note a retry is deliberately **not** itself a drop signal: a retry with no
matching record means the original never processed, and dropping it would turn a
slow turn into a lost one.

### 2. `app_mention` / `message` race (`inbound-reliability.ts`)

@-mentioning the bot in a channel produces **two** events for one human message,
in no guaranteed order. The old code resolved this with:

```ts
if (isMentioned && message.channel_type !== "im") return;
```

That is a coin flip, not a resolution. If the `app_mention` path bails for any
reason — gating, unbuildable memory, a throw — the turn is **silently lost**,
because the message twin already returned on the assumption the mention would
handle it.

Replaced with a lane state machine: both twins are admitted, and the message twin
awaits the mention's **actual terminal outcome**. `dispatched` suppresses it;
`skipped` or `failed` releases it to answer. If no mention arrives within a short
grace window (Slack emits the pair within milliseconds), the message proceeds —
that case is a guaranteed dropped turn on develop today.

### 3. Multi-workspace event isolation (`event-isolation.ts`)

Events were attributed to an account by **handler closure alone**. That is an
assumption, not a check, and it is wrong in every case where the socket is right
about the connection but wrong about the workspace: duplicate credentials across
accounts, Slack Connect / third-partyly-shared channels, org-wide install fanout, a
stale socket after a token swap.

`api_app_id` / `team_id` / `enterprise_id` from `auth.test` are now validated on
**every inbound family**, not just messages. Uniformity is the point: a foreign
`member_joined_channel` would otherwise widen *this* account's dynamic channel
allowlist, which is a concrete cross-tenant state corruption (test included).

Asymmetric on purpose: fail **closed** on present-and-different, fail **open** on
absent, with a carve-out so org-wide installs receiving sibling-workspace events
under the expected enterprise are not dropped.

### 4. Reconnect + liveness (`reconnect-policy.ts`)

Bolt retried everything forever, including `invalid_auth`, `token_revoked` and
`missing_scope` — credentials that can **never** succeed. That is an infinite
loop that burns rate limit, floods logs with identical failures, and worst of
all *looks* like recovery while the connector is permanently dead. Nobody is told.

Adds bounded jittered backoff (2s → 30s, ×1.8, 0.25 jitter, 12 attempts) for
recoverable errors, and an immediate bail with an actionable diagnosis for auth
errors. Auth is checked **before** the attempt ceiling so an operator with a
revoked token is told that, not "max attempts reached" (which would send them
looking at their network).

`lastEventAt` is tracked separately from `connected` and deliberately **not**
seeded on connect — connecting is not evidence of traffic, and seeding it would
mask exactly the wedged-socket case this exists to catch. Exposed via
`getHealth()` per account.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking) — four inbound correctness defects.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. All new behaviour is internal to the connector's inbound path; no config keys
added or changed. Rationale is documented in module-level comments.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-slack/src/service-inbound-reliability.test.ts`. It captures the
handlers off a fake Bolt `App` via `registerEventHandlers`, so every test drives
the **production wiring** and asserts on whether `processAgentMessage` /
`emitEvent` actually ran. A helper-level test would prove nothing about whether
the guards sit on the path Slack traffic takes.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun install
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-slack test        # 129/129 pass
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-slack typecheck   # zero errors
```

To reproduce the bidirectional proof (revert only the integration, keep the tests):

```bash
git checkout dc873211bee -- plugins/plugin-slack/src/service.ts
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-slack test src/service-inbound-reliability.test.ts
# 13 failed | 8 passed (21)
```

      ```
      CAPABILITY 1 - dedupe (event_id + lane TTL cache)
        admit(event_id=Ev_RETRY_1)              -> admitted   reason=new
        admit(event_id=Ev_RETRY_1, retry_num=1) -> REJECTED   reason=duplicate-event-id
        admit(ts=...100) / admit(ts=...200)     -> both admitted (distinct lanes)
        reaction_added Ev_REACTION x2           -> 1 emitEvent, 2nd duplicate-event-id
      CAPABILITY 2 - app_mention / message twin lane machine
        mention admitted, settle(dispatched)
          -> message admit  = REJECTED reason=twin-already-dispatched   (1 agent run)
        message admitted, mention admitted, settle(mention, dispatched)
          -> awaitMentionTwin = { proceed:false, reason:twin-dispatched } (1 agent run)
        message admitted, mention admitted, settle(mention, skipped)
          -> awaitMentionTwin = { proceed:true,  reason:twin-released }   (1 agent run)
        message admitted, mention admitted, settle(mention, failed)
          -> awaitMentionTwin = { proceed:true,  reason:twin-released }   (1 agent run)
        message admitted, no mention within grace
          -> awaitMentionTwin = { proceed:true,  reason:grace-expired }   (1 agent run)
        handler throw -> settle(failed) -> lane freed, redelivery re-admits
      CAPABILITY 3 - workspace isolation verdicts (expected A_APP / T_HOME)
        { api_app_id:A_APP,   team_id:T_OTHER } -> DROP field=team_id
        { api_app_id:A_OTHER, team_id:T_HOME  } -> DROP field=api_app_id
        { api_app_id:A_OTHER, team_id:T_OTHER } -> DROP field=api_app_id (first match)
        { event:{ team:T_OTHER } }              -> DROP field=team_id
        { enterprise_id:E_OTHER } vs E_HOME     -> DROP field=enterprise_id
        { team_id:T_SIBLING, enterprise_id:E_HOME } vs E_HOME -> ACCEPT (org-wide)
        { api_app_id:A_APP,   team_id:T_HOME  } -> ACCEPT
        { type:event_callback } (no identity)   -> ACCEPT (fail-open)
        identity {} (pre-auth.test)             -> ACCEPT (fail-open)
        foreign member_joined_channel(bot)      -> dynamicChannelIds unchanged
      CAPABILITY 4 - reconnect decisions + liveness states
        ECONNRESET      attempt 1  -> retry delayMs=2000
        ECONNRESET      attempt 2  -> retry delayMs=3600
        ECONNRESET      attempt 3  -> retry delayMs=6480
        ECONNRESET      attempt 50 -> clamped to maxMs=30000
        ECONNRESET      attempt 12 -> abort reason=exhausted
        token_revoked   attempt 1  -> abort reason=auth  (develop: retries forever)
        invalid_auth    attempt 1  -> abort reason=auth
        missing_scope   attempt 17 -> abort reason=auth  (auth beats exhaustion)
        err.data.error=token_revoked -> abort reason=auth (structured code read)
        "reinvalid_authorization"    -> recoverable      (word-boundary anchored)
        liveness: init                  -> connecting, lastEventAt=null
                  markConnected()       -> healthy,    lastEventAt=null (not seeded)
                  markEvent()           -> healthy
                  +stalenessMs silence  -> degraded
                  markEvent()           -> healthy
                  markDisconnected(err) -> disconnected, lastError=ECONNRESET
                  markPermanentFailure  -> failed
                  markConnected()       -> healthy, permanentFailure cleared
      ```

# Evidence Details

## Bidirectional proof (the core evidence)

Method: keep the new tests, revert **only** `service.ts` to develop
(`dc873211bee`), re-run. This isolates "is the guard actually wired into the
production path" from "does the helper work in a vacuum".

**Production-path suite (`service-inbound-reliability.test.ts`): 13 failed / 8
passed on unfixed develop → 21/21 pass with the fix.**

| # | Capability | Test | On develop | With fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Dedupe | runs the agent once when Slack redelivers the same message event | FAIL (ran 2×) | PASS |
| 1 | Dedupe | runs the agent once when the same `app_mention` is redelivered | FAIL (ran 2×) | PASS |
| 1 | Dedupe | dedupes redelivered non-message events by `event_id` | FAIL (emitted 2×) | PASS |
| 1 | Dedupe | still processes two genuinely distinct messages in one channel | pass | PASS |
| 2 | Race | does not drop the turn when the mention path **bails** | FAIL (0 runs — silent drop) | PASS |
| 2 | Race | does not drop the turn when the mention path **throws** | FAIL (0 runs — silent drop) | PASS |
| 2 | Race | answers a mention whose `app_mention` twin never arrives | FAIL (0 runs — silent drop) | PASS |
| 2 | Race | exactly once when the mention wins | pass | PASS |
| 2 | Race | exactly once when the message twin arrives first | pass | PASS |
| 2 | Race | both twins concurrently: no drop, no double | pass | PASS |
| 2 | Race | still answers DM mentions (no twin exists) | pass | PASS |
| 3 | Isolation | drops a message event from a foreign `team_id` | FAIL (accepted) | PASS |
| 3 | Isolation | drops a message event from a foreign `api_app_id` | FAIL (accepted) | PASS |
| 3 | Isolation | drops a foreign `app_mention` | FAIL (accepted) | PASS |
| 3 | Isolation | drops foreign events on **every** non-message family | FAIL (accepted) | PASS |
| 3 | Isolation | foreign `member_joined_channel` does not widen this account's allowlist | FAIL (state corrupted) | PASS |
| 3 | Isolation | accepts matching events (not a blanket deny) | pass | PASS |
| 3 | Isolation | accepts envelopes omitting identity fields (fail-open) | pass | PASS |
| 4 | Liveness | records `lastEventAt` only once traffic arrives | FAIL (no `getHealth`) | PASS |
| 4 | Liveness | counts a gated-out event as liveness | FAIL (no `getHealth`) | PASS |
| — | Wiring | binds every inbound family on the real Bolt app | pass | PASS |

The 8 that pass on develop are the **negative controls** — they confirm the new
guards do not over-block. A suite where everything failed on develop would be
consistent with a guard that simply drops all traffic; these are the tests that
rule that out.

### Reconnect (capability 4) — no develop counterpart

`reconnect-policy.ts` is new, so there is nothing on develop to fail against;
develop's behaviour is Bolt's default, which is "retry every error forever". The
32 tests in `reconnect-policy.test.ts` pin the distinction that did not
previously exist:

- `invalid_auth`, `token_revoked`, `token_expired`, `account_inactive`,
  `missing_scope`, `not_authed`, `org_login_required`, `app_uninstalled` →
  **abort at attempt 1**, no reconnect (on develop: infinite churn).
- `ECONNRESET`, `socket hang up`, `ENOTFOUND`, `ratelimited`, `server_error` →
  retry with bounded jittered backoff.
- Auth reported **before** exhaustion, so the error names the real cause.
- Word-boundary anchored so `reinvalid_authorization` is not matched as
  `invalid_auth`; structured `err.data.error` read alongside message text.
- Liveness: connect does not seed `lastEventAt`; a connected-but-silent socket
  reports `degraded`; a permanent bail reports `failed`.

# Rebase order vs #17462

**This PR should land AFTER #17462.** Both touch `service.ts`; #17462 is
concurrently reworking gating, policy, and event classification in the same file.

Deconfliction already applied:

- Branched from `develop` (`dc873211bee`), **not** from the #17462 branch.
- All new logic lives in three standalone modules — `inbound-reliability.ts`,
  `event-isolation.ts`, `reconnect-policy.ts` — with **zero** imports into
  files #17462 edits (`accounts.ts`, `allowlist.ts`).
- `service.ts` touch-points are deliberately minimal and sit in different regions
  than the gating rework:

| Region | This PR | #17462 |
|---|---|---|
| `SlackAccountRuntime` type | +3 fields (`identity`, `reliability`, `liveness`) | +1 field (`channelConfigs`) |
| `initializeAccount` | identity capture after `auth.test`, socket lifecycle | `channelConfigs` init |
| `registerEventHandlers` | isolation + liveness + dedupe wrapper per family | untouched |
| `handleMessage` | admission + twin resolution around the existing body | gate replacement mid-body |
| `handleAppMention` | admission + settle around the existing body | gate insertion before body |

The `handleMessage` / `handleAppMention` overlaps are the only real ones and are
small. Concretely: #17462 replaces the mention-check block with
`resolveSlackInboundGate`; this PR wraps that same region in admission/settle.
On rebase, keep #17462's gate call verbatim inside the `try` block this PR adds.

Happy to hold this as draft until #17462 merges, then rebase and re-post counts.
Isolation (capability 3) is also the mechanism that makes #17462's blocker-6
workspace-isolation evidence **provable rather than asserted** — the two are
complementary, and the isolation module is usable from the gating lane
independently if that ordering is preferred.

# Known gaps / failures

- Monorepo-wide `bun run verify` was not run to completion; this worktree carries
  unrelated pre-existing dirt (binary asset + benchmark manifest churn) not
  produced by this change. Scoped verification for the touched package is clean:
  plugin suite 129/129, typecheck zero errors, biome clean across all 21 files.
- Gap-audit item 6 (missing `thread_ts` recovery via `conversations.history`) was
  listed as optional for this slice and is **not** included. The keying handles
  the unresolved case correctly today via the `maybe-thread` prefix, so an
  unresolved reply is isolated rather than mis-laned; actively resolving it needs
  an API round-trip with its own cache and belongs in its own PR.
- Rate limiting / 429 handling (audit item 5) is out of scope — separate slice.
- Reconnect observation hooks into Bolt's receiver socket client, an internal
  shape. If a future Bolt release moves it, `getSlackSocketEmitter` returns
  `null` and reconnect handling degrades to Bolt's current behaviour rather than
  throwing at startup. Deliberate: the failure mode is "no worse than today".
30:T15e0,Closes #17527

## What this fixes

The benchmark process previously used a fixed 120-second `Promise.race`, did not abort the losing message turn, and exited without owning runtime/database shutdown. The same boot path also launched dynamic imports, plugin registration, cache warmups, maintenance DB work, and a five-second skill-command timer without a lifecycle owner. A benchmark could therefore stop reporting while work continued, or close SQL while startup jobs were still reading and writing.

This changes ownership instead of adding another deadline:

- removes the benchmark timeout option and timer race from both CLI entry points;
- passes a process-owned `AbortSignal` through the real message-service path;
- handles SIGINT/SIGTERM through cancellation plus normal shutdown rather than `process.exit()`;
- preserves partial streamed output while reporting success only for a completed, valid response;
- drains deferred imports, plugin registration, hooks, warmups, and tracked post-delivery work before adapter close;
- removes arbitrary deferred-plugin, trajectory-drain, hot-reload, and plugin-initialization timeouts;
- makes memory telemetry flush single-flight and awaited, without intercepting SIGTERM;
- removes plugin-openai's detached startup `GET /models` validation request;
- moves workbench migration, attachment backfill scheduling, and media-GC scheduling into a runtime-owned maintenance service that starts after SQL migrations;
- registers task workers before TaskService starts, then creates their idempotent queue rows only when SQL is ready;
- registers loaded skills as commands during dependency-ordered plugin initialization instead of an unowned five-second timer.

## Live Cerebras proof

Fresh real run on this branch with Cerebras `gemma-4-31b`:

- exact response: `BENCHMARK-OWNER-ALLOWED-FINAL`
- benchmark response: **929 ms**
- model stage: **853 ms**
- full boot: **1,883 ms**
- process wall including boot and clean shutdown: **4,280 ms**
- prompt/completion: **4,142 / 52 tokens**
- cache read: **3,328 prompt tokens (80.35%)**
- native trajectory: `finished`, one physical model request
- after process exit: zero active trajectory rows, zero post-close DB operations, zero pre-migration task reads, zero unhandled rejections

Provider work remained parallel. The 13 observed provider durations were 1.32–15.30 ms; the slowest was FACTS at 15.30 ms. The largest boot cost was local SQL registration at 1,113 ms, followed by runtime initialization at 344 ms and blocking plugin resolution at 248 ms.

[Machine-readable live evidence](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-6/pr-17527-live-evidence.json) (`sha256:d5c2a87ac0c42f4d703a0d35a260f3bf3e5c1a43a2dff88c0269551c07eae8e3`).

### Telemetry caveat discovered by this run

The native trajectory proves one Cerebras request, but the DB aggregate reports two LLM calls and exactly doubles token totals. That is a separate double-recording defect between generic runtime instrumentation and plugin-level wire telemetry, not a second network request. It is explicitly recorded in the evidence and is being fixed independently so this PR stays about task/lifecycle ownership.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces active `--min-tasks` / `MIN_TEST_TASKS` count floors with
  `--require-work`: zero-task, empty-shard, and all-skipped required selections
  exit nonzero.
- Uses strict JUnit reconciliation for Bun and Vitest tasks so `test.skip` cannot
  satisfy required work.
- Validates exhaustive plans against live package manifests, executable scripts,
  unique identities, and exact derived summaries.
- Deletes the frozen Windows command inventory and resolves every parsed matrix
  command to its live entrypoint or package script.
- Adds an always-emitted develop-PR `test` context that runs changed owning-package
  tests when present and always runs the complete repository script inventory.
- Replaces historical coverage-count assertions with nonempty or exact
  source-derived ownership contracts.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and CI reliability improvement; no product runtime or UI behavior changes.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is needed. Workflow comments, CLI help, and
manifest comments were updated with the shipped source contracts.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Run `bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/run-all-tests-vacuous-green-guard.test.ts`.
2. Run `node packages/scripts/ci-workflow-invariants.mjs`.
3. Run `node packages/scripts/ci-full-matrix-proof.mjs` and
   `node packages/scripts/ci-windows-command-coverage-contract.mjs`.
4. Run `bun run test:scripts`, then `bun run verify`.

33:T1ce3,## Summary

Closes #17185.

One logical managed Docker allocation now releases exactly one
`docker_nodes.allocated_count` slot, across retries, crashes, already-absent
responses, suspend/delete sequences, and late provider completion.

`DockerSandboxProvider.stopWithPolicy` used to decrement the node counter
unconditionally at the end of every teardown. Remote teardown is retryable and
treats "No such container" as success, but the sandbox row keeps its node
locator until the row itself is deleted — so a retry after any post-stop failure
(credential revocation, the row-delete CAS, job-status persistence) decremented
the same slot again, and the slot it freed belonged to a **live sibling** on that
node. `GREATEST(count - 1, 0)` hid the underflow rather than preventing it.
Suspend had the same shape: it stops and decrements while keeping the locator,
so a later delete repeated the decrement with no retry involved.

Capacity ownership is now declared per operation, matching the seam
`stopOnSpecificNodeWithPolicy` already had:

- `agent_sandboxes.deletion_allocation_counted` (migration 0187) records whether
  a deletion generation still owns a counted slot. It mirrors the
  `replacement_cleanup_allocation_counted` mechanism this table already uses for
  the replacement path.
- `stop()` passes `releaseCapacity: false` — the deletion generation hands the
  slot back itself, exactly once, via a CAS that flips ownership and decrements
  the node in one transaction. `stopForReplacement()` passes `true`, so suspend,
  shutdown, sleep, warm-claim retire and ghost cleanup are unchanged.
- The release only runs when the container is **proven** gone. A reachable stop
  failure and a bounded-timeout abandon both keep ownership, so a container that
  may still be running never has its slot handed to the scheduler.
- The orphan reconciler releases the slot in the same sweep that reaps the
  container — the one place absence is proven. That is what stops an abandoned
  `deletion_failed` row from holding a slot forever once
  `reEnqueueFailedDeletions` hits its circuit breaker.
- Workload reconciliation counts a deletion row exactly while it owns a slot.
  `NULL` falls through to the previous status rule, so pre-migration rows behave
  byte-identically.

`holdsCountedNodeSlot` is derived from `TERMINAL_SANDBOX_STATUSES` rather than
relisting statuses, so it cannot drift from the set `syncAllocatedCounts`
recomputes against — a divergence there would release a slot twice.

## Testing

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/__tests__/deletion-allocation-ownership.test.ts`
(24 cases) drives the real repository CAS and the real reconciliation query
against in-process PGlite with the real Drizzle schema and nothing mocked, so the
actual SQL executes. It carries a loud `expect(pgliteReady).toBe(true)` guard so
a PGlite failure can never masquerade as a vacuous green.

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/__tests__/docker-sandbox-stop-no-capacity-mutation.test.ts`
(5 cases) drives the real `DockerSandboxProvider` with only the SSH transport
scripted, so the real already-absent / unreachable-abandon / hard-failure
classification decides each path.

Covered: release exactly once across retries; a live sibling keeping its slot;
concurrent release; stale generation, stale node, and cross-organization CAS
rejection; suspended rows that never owned a slot; the `allocated_count > 0`
underflow guard; ownership initialization for running/suspended/unplaced agents
through the real `ProvisioningJobService.enqueueAgentDeleteOnce`, including
recovery re-enqueue inheriting the original answer; reaper release, its
idempotency and its node fence; and all four reconciliation states including
pre-migration `NULL`.

Verified the tests actually catch the defect: restoring the old unconditional
decrement fails 4 of the 5 provider cases.

      — real PGlite rows through the real repository CAS: a node at
      `allocated_count = 2` carrying the agent under deletion plus a LIVE
      sibling. Release #1 takes it to 1 and spends ownership; releases #2 and #3
      (the post-stop-failure retry and a recovery sweep) return false and leave
      it at 1. Before this change the third read 0 — the sibling's slot. The same
      transitions are asserted in the 24 database cases; migration 0187 applies
      via `pushSchema` there.

## Notes for review

- Migration 0187 adds a nullable column with no default and no backfill —
  metadata-only on PG11+. No CHECK constraint pairing it with
  `deletion_attempt_id`: `ADD CONSTRAINT` full-scans `agent_sandboxes` under
  ACCESS EXCLUSIVE, and the pairing is structural (every writer sets both in one
  UPDATE). Same trade-off migration 0185 documents.
- The column is registered in `ensure-agent-sandbox-schema.ts` and every reader
  and writer calls the ensure guard, because
  `deploy-eliza-provisioning-worker.yml` has `needs: determine-env` and no
  `migrate-db` gate.
- `NULL` is a deliberate third state. A pre-migration deletion in flight leaves
  that node reading one slot fuller than it is until `syncAllocatedCounts` next
  runs — the safe direction, since schedulers gate on
  `allocated_count < capacity`, so the error can only under-pack a node.

# Risks

Medium. This changes release CI ordering, expands OS validation to PRs targeting `develop`, adds the missing UEFI optical-media boot path to the pinned Tails live-build fork, and makes the VM smoke probe use Tails' gated test channel instead of racing a serial login. A malformed, incomplete, non-bootable, or unhealthy ISO now fails earlier and more often by design. Runtime and UI code are untouched.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Runs the live Tails APT snapshot resolver immediately after checkout, before cache restoration, tool setup, or workspace installation.
- Registers the ISO workflow with the OS validation workflow for `develop` PRs.
- Replaces the previous filename grep with a parsed YAML contract covering canonical amd64-only release, exact snapshot-map export, static-only non-amd64 work, shipped-bootloader firmware validation, retained diagnostics, SLSA provenance, complete runtime SBOM, and fail-closed artifacts.
- Adds 20 baseline/mutation tests and wires the existing exact-clean-pin test into `static-smoke.sh`.
- Packages Tails' signed shim and GRUB loaders into a deterministic FAT image, then applies a fail-closed compatibility patch to the exact pinned live-build fork so xorriso advertises it as a UEFI El Torito entry alongside unchanged ISOLINUX BIOS boot.
- Uses Tails' existing VM-only `org.tails.remote_shell.0` channel, gated by the explicit `autotest_never_use_this_option` kernel flag, to release GDM and check the real `amnesia` user service plus `127.0.0.1:31337/api/health`. No production authentication or direct-kernel bypass is introduced.
- Uses acknowledged, short readiness requests that report the actual user-bus, service, curl, and health-payload states. Transient states retry; protocol failures fail immediately; only the guest-produced service-active/health-ready marker passes.
- Makes the builder cache key cover the boot hook and live-build patch.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking CI/release reliability and bootability change).

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated `packages/os/linux/docs/build-infrastructure.md` with the signed EFI image, compatibility patch, and dual-El-Torito build contract.

## Backend + frontend logs

The first real build reached ISO assembly after 58m55s and produced a 2.06 GB artifact. Manual review of retained xorriso diagnostics found one bootable BIOS entry and no UEFI entry; the workflow failed observably with `ERROR: ISO has no bootable UEFI El Torito image` and the test was not weakened.

The next real build reported bootable BIOS `/isolinux/isolinux.bin` and UEFI `/boot/grub/efi.img` entries, then reached Linux userspace through SeaBIOS. It failed before OVMF because the harness treated Tails' graphical `login` flag as a passwordless serial-getty contract. The repair switched to Tails' own VM-only test channel without changing authentication.

Run 30717357426 built the real ISO in 1h06m59s, retained both boot entries, reached Linux and the `amnesia` session through SeaBIOS, and received `[1, "success"]` from the gated Tails remote channel. The old single guest-side retry command and ten-minute host bound expired as GDM/user@1000 became ready. The current repair uses short acknowledged requests with observable `bus/service/health` states and a 15-minute per-firmware bound; it does not relax the service-active or `ready:true` pass condition.

The current run checks out evidence SHA `73cf36ebccc0`, which is exact PR head `d9a7b2b4c461` plus only the isolated `packageManager` change from unpublished Bun 1.4.0 to published Bun 1.3.14. `git diff d9a7b2b4c461..73cf36ebccc0` contains that one line and nothing else.

# What this does

- Removes the global archive overlay from root postinstall, the aggregate `artifacts-manifest.json`, `packages/scripts/sync-artifacts.mjs`, the `sync:artifacts` command, and every `ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC` workflow escape hatch (including `cerebras-chat-flow-live.yml`, added to develop after the original PR).
- `install:light` becomes a compatibility alias for the standard install.
- Deletes the 501-line archive-owned root `.gitignore` block; keeps develop's newer worktree-ignore block.
- Adds a fail-closed source-owned artifact authority contract (`packages/scripts/artifact-authority-contract.mjs` + 12 tests), runnable on demand via `bun run audit:artifact-authority` and enforced by `test.yml`'s post-merge contract lane (per the standing no-per-PR-ratchet policy from #16304's close, it is NOT in `verify` or the develop-pr lint job).
- Documents the explicit producer/scoped-consumer rule in root `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`.

# Rebase decisions (vs the original #16304 commit)

- `package.json` `verify`: kept develop's current chain, inserted only `audit:artifact-authority`; did not resurrect the removed ratchet gates or `audit:test-realness`.
- `develop-pr.yml`: contract step moved to the surviving `lint` job.
- New develop references retired: `cerebras-chat-flow-live.yml` env line removed; stale comments updated in `cloud-tests.yml`, `test-cloud-run.mjs`, `test-cloud-run-preflight.self-test.mjs`.
- Fix folded in: the contract CLI `isMain` guard now realpaths `argv[1]` (macOS `/var → /private/var` tmp symlink made the CLI-boundary test exit 0).

# Risks

Low by construction and off by default in effect: the budget's default ceiling is the shared canonical restorable limit, so it refuses exactly what the downstream consumer would already have rejected — a capture that succeeds today and is restorable still succeeds. The behavior change is *where* the refusal happens (in the producer, before the heap is spent) rather than *what* is refused.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Cloud's restorable-size check is strictly **downstream** of the agent having already assembled and serialized the whole payload. It bounds what Cloud *retains*; it never bounds what the agent's own heap burns getting there. `createAgentSnapshot` ran its five captures concurrently with **no size awareness at all**:

- file capture reads a file and base64-encodes it — ~2.33× the file size transiently (Buffer + base64 string), ~1.33× durably;
- the **media** set has no include predicate whatsoever, so the whole content-addressed store is base64'd into the manifest;
- `stat.size` was already read and never compared to anything.

So a large agent can exhaust its container mid-capture. The memory watchdog then force-restarts it, and the lifecycle sites that snapshot before **upgrade / shutdown / sleep** silently degrade to a stale or missing backup — the failure this issue is about, one layer earlier than the Cloud fix.

Adds a produce-side budget:

- charged **as bytes are produced**, so an over-budget capture is refused at the first byte past the line instead of after everything is resident;
- file entries are refused from the **declared `stat` size before the read** — charging afterwards is charging after the allocation;
- a file-count ceiling mirroring the hydration-side cap;
- an optional `AbortSignal`, threaded through the directory walk so a cancelled capture stops descending;
- a typed `AgentSnapshotBudgetExceededError`, so "this state is too large to snapshot" is distinguishable from a transport/disk failure (the former is deterministic; retrying identical state cannot help).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (source-process memory safety on the snapshot path).

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`agent-backup.test.ts` → `describe("source-side snapshot budget")`. The load-bearing case is **"refuses BEFORE reading the oversized file, not after materializing it"**: it wraps `fs.readFile` and asserts the oversized path is **never read**. A post-read charge would pass a naive size assertion while still having paid the allocation — that test is what separates the two.

Also covered: byte-budget refusal, file-count refusal, an already-aborted signal, and a within-budget capture still succeeding unchanged.

## Detailed testing steps

None: automated. `packages/agent` suite **9/9** (5 new), `tsgo --noEmit` clean, biome clean.

## Known gaps / failures

**The Postgres single-query peak is now bounded** — this was left open in the first commit and is closed in `af967f6`. The two unbounded shapes (memories-joined embeddings, generic owner-column) read in **keyset batches** ordered by primary key, charging the budget per batch, so the peak is one batch and an oversized table is refused mid-walk. Keyset rather than OFFSET: OFFSET re-scans from the start each page, and a snapshot taken while the agent is live would skip or duplicate rows as they shift. This is the **no-new-dependency** option — I did not add `pg-cursor` to the agent container image, since that would have been a call about the image rather than about this code.

Two honest remainders:
- A table with **no primary key** keeps its single read (there is nothing to walk); the post-read charge still bounds the assembled snapshot. Documented at the call site. In practice these are the small tables.
- **Source-memory profiling evidence** (RSS during a capture of a genuinely huge state dir) is not attached — it needs a realistic large agent rather than a fixture. The deterministic reproductions are in the test lane.
