37:T19db,## Problem

`plugins/plugin-slack` handles `reaction_added` / `reaction_removed`, but the handlers took `_event`, **threw the reaction data away**, and emitted only the plugin-local `SLACK_REACTION_ADDED` / `SLACK_REACTION_REMOVED` with `buildEventPayload(accountId)` — a payload carrying no emoji, no user, no message reference.

Nothing in core consumes those plugin-local names. So Slack emoji signal never reached the agent at all: a consumer that wanted to count 👍/👎 had to poll `reactions.get`.

`plugin-discord` already bridges reactions to core's `EventType.REACTION_RECEIVED`. This PR gives Slack the same core-event contract, so a consumer counts reactions without knowing which platform produced them.

## Change

New `plugins/plugin-slack/src/reaction-bridge.ts`, wired into **both** reaction handlers on the real registered path (`app.event("reaction_added" | "reaction_removed")` in `registerEventHandlers`) — not a helper that could drift onto dead code.

- **Core contract matched exactly.** Emits `[SLACK_REACTION_ADDED, EventType.REACTION_RECEIVED]` on add, with a `MessagePayload` carrying the reaction `Memory`, mirroring `plugin-discord`'s `handleReaction`.
- **Same rooms as messages.** Reuses the service's own `createUniqueUuid` / `stringToUuid` helpers (`slack-room`, `slack-user`, `slack` keys, account-scoped) via an injected host adapter, so reactions land on identical room/entity UUIDs rather than a parallel set.
- **Thread recovery.** A Slack reaction event carries `channel` + `ts` but *never* `thread_ts`, so naive mapping drops every threaded reaction into the parent channel room, detached from the message it reacted to. The bridge resolves the target message memory (its id is deterministic from `ts`) and reuses its `roomId`, falling back to the channel room when the agent never saw the message.
- **Payload:** emoji name, mapped room UUID, reactor entity UUID, target message ref (`targetMessageTs` + deterministic `targetMessageId`, plus `content.inReplyTo`), resolved reacted-to text when known.
- **Back-compat preserved.** `SLACK_REACTION_*` still fire, now with the populated payload the `SlackReactionPayload` type always declared but never filled in. `SlackReactionPayload` now extends `MessagePayload` so one payload satisfies both event names.

### Deliberate: removal does not reach core

Core declares exactly one reaction event, `EventType.REACTION_RECEIVED` (`packages/core/src/types/events.ts`); there is no `REACTION_REMOVED`. `plugin-discord` resolves this by emitting `[DiscordEventTypes.REACTION_RECEIVED, EventType.REACTION_RECEIVED]` on add but only `[DiscordEventTypes.REACTION_REMOVED]` on remove.

We match that rather than "improving" on it. Emitting `REACTION_RECEIVED` for a Slack removal would make a platform-agnostic vote counter **double-count on Slack and not on Discord** — worse than the missing event. Removals still get the full enriched payload on `SLACK_REACTION_REMOVED`. Closing the asymmetry properly means adding `EventType.REACTION_REMOVED` to core and updating both plugins together; that is a core-types change, deliberately out of scope.

Likewise the bridge does not call `createMemory` itself — core's `reactionReceivedHandler` persists the reaction memory, same as Discord — so an add is stored once and a removal is not stored at all.

## Proof (bidirectional)

`src/reaction-bridge.test.ts` (14 tests) drives the **real handlers** bound by `registerEventHandlers`, asserting core `emitEvent` receives `EventType.REACTION_RECEIVED`.

Same test file, run against unmodified `develop` sources vs. with the fix:

| | Result |
|---|---|
| **develop** (`service.ts`/`types.ts` reverted, `reaction-bridge.ts` removed) | **2 pass / 12 fail** |
| **with fix** | **14 pass / 0 fail** (42 assertions) |

The 12 failures on develop are the substantive ones: no core `REACTION_RECEIVED` emitted, payload carries no emoji/ts/channel/reactor, no reaction `Memory`, no `inReplyTo`, thread routing absent, allowlist ungated, malformed events still emit. The 2 that pass on develop are the handler-registration check and the back-compat `SLACK_REACTION_ADDED` presence check — correctly, since those already worked.

**Full plugin suite: 52 pass / 0 fail across 6 files.**

Core built first (`packages/core`, `bun run build` ✅), then `plugin-slack` `bun run typecheck` → **zero errors**, `biome check` clean on all four touched files.

### Note on the room-mapping test

The room-mapping assertion initially passed vacuously: the harness stubbed `runtime.getRoom` with a blanket `mockResolvedValue({ id: "room-existing" })`, which made `ensureRoomExists` short-circuit on its `getRoom(roomId)` hit and return a room whose id never came from `createUniqueUuid` — so the mapping under test was never exercised. Replaced with a real room store keyed by UUID, so an unseen channel misses, falls through to the real `getRoomId` + `createRoom` path, and the test now additionally asserts `createRoom` was called once with the expected id/channel/source.

## Rebase order / collisions

Two lanes are concurrently editing this plugin:

- `sol-slack-rework-17462` — `service.ts` / `accounts.ts` / `allowlist.ts`
- `sol-slack-reliability-a` — inbound-reliability / event-isolation / reconnect-policy modules

This PR's `service.ts` footprint is deliberately surgical: the two reaction handler bodies plus one new private `reactionBridgeHost()` adapter. All substantive logic lives in the new `reaction-bridge.ts`, which neither lane touches.

**Rebase after the `17462` rework lands.** Opened as **draft** for that reason.

39:T1a90,## Problem

Live incident (production Discord bot, trajectory `tj-69d82bb89ebb69`): asked **"how many times have i mentioned bitcoin in this channel?"**, the agent replied *"i don't see any mention of bitcoin in the recent messages i can see."* — answering a beyond-window history question from the ~20-message visible window, and denying a capability it has.

Root cause is a **false capability denial baked into the Stage-1 prompt**, which the model obeyed faithfully:

1. `createV5MessageContextObject`'s `current_turn_boundary` instruction hardcoded *"the prior_message blocks are the only window you have, and there is no separate chat-history search tool."*
2. `messageHandlerTemplate` scripted the matching canned denial for a window miss.

But the runtime **does** have that tool: the `MEMORY` action's `op:search` filters the stored `messages` record by room + query. Because Stage-1 never sees the action catalog (only the `available_contexts` list), the routing hints on that action could never reach this decision — the turn ended at Stage-1 with `contexts:["simple"]`, no `toolSearch`, no planner, no tool. The hardcoded denial also directly contradicted two of the template's own rules (`never deny a capability when a context is available`; `never simple when … "how many X"`).

## Fix

The boundary instruction now branches on the turn's **actual capability surface** — whether the role-filtered `availableContexts` (already passed by every caller) includes the `memory` context:

- **Memory context present:** state that the visible blocks are the most recent window of a longer stored conversation, and route beyond-window recall/count questions to the memory context (`requiresTool`) so the stored record is actually searched. Every anti-fabrication guarantee is retained verbatim (never claim a search a tool did not return).
- **Memory context absent:** the previous text is **byte-identical** (verified: 1161 chars, string-equal), preserving the 2026-05-25 fabricated-search guard for minimal runtimes and sub-USER roles.

Supporting: the template's canned denial defers to the ground-truth capability rule instead of dictating "no such tool", and the `memory` context catalog description names conversation recall so Stage-1 can select it. The recent-messages provider also names its window (`most recent N`) rather than presenting itself as the whole conversation.

**No user-message-text inspection anywhere** — the branch reads only the registered, role-filtered context surface. (A prose-regex intent gate was removed as a bug in #17500; this deliberately does not reintroduce that pattern.)

### Known trade (raised in adversarial review)

The gate proves *context registration*, not *action registration*: `DEFAULT_CONTEXT_DEFINITIONS` seeds `memory` in core, while the MEMORY action ships in `@elizaos/agent`. A deployment building on `AgentRuntime` with custom plugins and no memory action would get the escalating branch. Two things bound the damage: the Stage-1 catalog **already** advertises the memory context on exactly those runtimes (the boundary now merely agrees with the catalog the model already sees), and the anti-fabrication rule still forbids claiming an unrun search — so the degraded outcome is one wasted planner escalation with an honest reply, not a fabricated answer. Tightening the gate to also require a registered memory-context action is a reasonable follow-up; it would diverge the boundary from the catalog, so it belongs with catalog action-gating rather than here. Happy to make that change if preferred.

## Testing

- Two new tests in `message-runtime-stage1.test.ts` assert the rendered Stage-1 prompt for both branches; the memory-branch assertions fail on pre-fix code by construction (the removed sentence exists verbatim in develop; the new strings exist nowhere pre-fix).
- Suites green: stage1 **104**, prompts **38**, context/catalog/registry/renderer **63**, message + planner lanes **121**, provider lanes **40**. `tsgo --noEmit` clean, biome clean on all touched files.
- Adversarial review verified byte-identity of the untouched branch, all three call sites of `createV5MessageContextObject`, no prompt-cache regression (the boundary event is `stable:false`, so the KV-prewarmed stable prefix is unaffected), and that guest/stranger turns keep the old denial via role filtering.
- **Live before/after on a production Discord bot** — see evidence.

3a:T9c5,## Summary

Builds on the merged retain/restore safety work in #17180 and completes the producer-side half of #17172 without replaying any of that now-upstream cloud history.

- bounds agent snapshot capture before file and PGlite payloads are materialized;
- reserves capacity across concurrent capture components so siblings cannot collectively overspend the limit;
- enforces the restore-side file-count ceiling at capture time;
- reads agent-scoped PostgreSQL tables in bounded keyset pages and fails rather than returning a partial dump when pagination cannot advance;
- charges in-memory character and legacy config projections;
- validates the exact final JSON wire size, including metadata and structural overhead, against the shared 128 MiB restorable ceiling;
- returns typed `ElizaError` failures for producer budget breaches and terminal restore-consent refusals.

The three original producer-budget commits retain @standujar's authorship. The final commit contains review fixes found while consolidating the branch onto current `develop`.

Supersedes #17214. Builds on #17180.

3c:T112c,## Problem

The Stage-1 message prompt placed volatile provider output, especially CURRENT_TIME, before the large append-only conversation tail. Cerebras caches exact 128-token prefix blocks, so this ordering invalidated almost the entire reusable prefix every turn. The live Gemma 4 baseline reused only 41.57% of prompt tokens at p50 despite a stable system prompt.

The latency harness also summarized wall/model/provider time without preserving the exact production model input or provider-reported prompt-cache and queue timings. That made a green-looking report possible without proving what the model actually received.

## Fix

- Keep the stable system prompt first, then order the user message as current-turn boundary, chronological prior dialogue, volatile provider output, and the final current message.
- Preserve the semantic current-turn invariant without positional above/below wording.
- Capture the exact RESPONSE_HANDLER input for every warmup, sample, and cancellation invocation, including prompt segments and cache options but only allow-listed fields.
- Report prompt, cached, uncached, and cache-rate token distributions from Cerebras usage; missing cache telemetry is an explicit error rather than a fabricated zero.
- Preserve privacy-safe nested cached-token and provider time_info fields in the cloud probe.
- Give every related direct, gateway, and paired request a stable prompt_cache_key.
- Add observations only. There is no latency or cache threshold, ratchet, timeout gate, or changed-production-code test policy.

Fixes #17509

The report embeds that Git head and `treeClean: true`; the harness now refuses to emit evidence from a dirty tracked tree.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Branched from the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts.
- [x] Targeted verification recorded under **Testing** (changed-package tests + typecheck).

# Risks

Low, and strictly in the safe direction: the retain budget only ever gets **smaller** (256 MiB → 128 MiB), never larger. Nothing that restores today stops restoring; what stops is retaining a payload that could never be restored. Small manual/auto v1 backups are untouched, and no heap or body limit is raised (the issue's rejected-shortcuts list is respected).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The production canary failed because Cloud **retained** a snapshot larger than anything on the restore path can **consume**:

| Side | Constant | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| retain | `SNAPSHOT_MAX_RAW_BYTES` (`eliza-sandbox.ts`) | **256 MiB** | canonical (128 MiB), env-clamped |
| restore | `MAX_BACKUP_BODY_BYTES` (`packages/agent/src/api/server.ts`) | 128 MiB | canonical |
| reconstruct | `MAX_RECONSTRUCTED_BACKUP_CHAIN_BYTES` (`agent-sandboxes.ts`) | 128 MiB | canonical |

A 128–256 MiB snapshot was therefore retained, authorized a cutover, and could never be restored.

- New `packages/shared/src/agent-backup-limits.ts` holds **`MAX_RESTORABLE_AGENT_BACKUP_BYTES` (128 MiB)** — the one canonical v1 restorable wire size. `@elizaos/shared` is already a dependency of **both** `packages/agent` and `packages/cloud/shared`, so retain, restore and reconstruction import one source of truth with no import cycle. This mirrors the existing `chat-upload-limits.ts` precedent (a leaf limits module both sides import so they cannot drift).
- `resolveRetainableAgentBackupBytes()` resolves the `ELIZA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_RAW_BYTES` operator override **clamped to the canonical limit**. The override keeps its real purpose (lowering the budget for a staging soak or a constrained worker) but can no longer re-open the divergence by configuration alone — which it could before.
- The breach still fails exactly where it already did: **while streaming, before retention** (`readBodyWithinBudget`), so a too-large snapshot is never persisted and never authorizes a cutover.

## Deliberately NOT changed: the verifier's decrypt budget

`DEFAULT_MAX_DECRYPT_BYTES` (256 MiB, `agent-backup-verifier.ts`) is left alone on purpose. It is a **per-cycle aggregate** budget spanning a batch of backups, not a per-backup wire size — a different axis. Lowering it to 128 MiB would be a *regression*: one max-size backup would consume an entire cycle, so the sweep would verify a single backup per cycle. At 256 MiB a max-size (128 MiB) backup always fits with room to spare, and `chargeBudget` already distinguishes `payload-exceeds-cycle-budget` (permanent, stop sampling) from `cycle-budget-exhausted` (transient, defer) — so the "can never verify" case is already handled correctly.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (correctness: removes a retainable-but-unrestorable backup state).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation; the new module documents the contract at its definition.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/shared/src/agent-backup-limits.test.ts` — it pins the whole contract: the canonical ceiling is 128 MiB; an override that **lowers** is honored; an override that **raises** (including the exact pre-fix 256 MiB that produced the unrestorable canary snapshot) is clamped; malformed/non-positive fall back; exact-limit passes and limit+1 clamps; and a final invariant case asserting no input can ever resolve a retain budget above the restorable ceiling — the guard that fails if someone raises one side without the other.

## Detailed testing steps

None: automated tests are acceptable. `packages/shared` vitest lane: **7 pass / 0 fail** on the new suite. The pre-existing budget tests in `eliza-sandbox.test.ts` (from #16639/#16647) pass explicit budgets into `readBodyWithinBudget`/`assertSnapshotExpandedBudgets`, so they are unaffected by the default change.

# Evidence Details

The load-bearing new property is the **clamp**, not the constant: before this PR an operator could set `ELIZA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_RAW_BYTES=268435456` and silently recreate the exact canary failure. The test asserts that specific value now resolves to the canonical 128 MiB, plus a general invariant over a range of inputs so the property holds for anything an operator types.

## Known gaps / failures

Only §1 of #17172 is addressed — §2 (legacy→v2 standby bridge), §3 (additive v2 chunked backup protocol) and §4 (rollout) remain open on the card and are the substantial architecture. This PR makes the current v1 path *safe* (no unrestorable retention) but does not make large agents *backupable* — a >128 MiB agent will now fail cleanly before cutover instead of retaining a dead snapshot, which is the intended §1 behavior ("budget failures are typed, observable, non-retryable, and occur before cutover") and exactly why §3 exists.

# Known gaps / failures

Draft until exact-head desktop/mobile screenshots and intro video are captured and manually reviewed. The local macOS snapshot lane correctly fails closed because no macOS baseline exists; CI must prove the repository-supported platform lane.41:T10fa,## What — the root fix for the #17249 class

**Depends on #17260** (branched on top of it; merge that first — the two commits beyond it are this PR).

`getAgentForLifecycleMutation` was a raw `SELECT * FOR UPDATE`: raw drizzle rows carry timestamptz as **strings** despite the `AgentSandbox` type (drizzle installs identity type parsers and re-maps only typed builders). #17260 fixed the delete crash at two call sites; this types the read itself, healing the whole class.

## What the exhaustive consumer audit found (every date usage classified, ~40 sites)

Three more **live production defects**, invisible to the unit suite because every mock feeds `Date` fixtures:

- **`agent_sleep` was 100% broken** — the commit CAS called `.getTime()` on a raw string (`updated_at` is NOT NULL, so the optional chain never saved it). Every sleep failed at commit; nothing was destroyed, the job just always failed.
- **Managed-launch env updates crashed** — `eq(updated_at, rec.updated_at)` pushed the raw string through the column encoder → `value.toISOString is not a function` → the freshly minted credential was revoked by the compensating teardown.
- **Warm-claimed agents could never complete an upgrade/downgrade** — the in-transaction CAS consults `hasReadyWarmClaimCredential`, whose contract checks `warm_claim_attested_at instanceof Date`; on a raw string that is `false` for every warm-claimed agent → "Agent changed during upgrade", blue torn down, retries exhausted. The same helper answered correctly earlier on the same path from a typed row — two gates silently contradicting each other. **This PR flips that behavior on purpose: warm-claimed upgrades start working.**

## The microsecond trap (why the naive fix was dangerous)

The typed mapping truncates to **milliseconds**; ~20 raw `updated_at = NOW()` writers store **microseconds**. Two exact-equality fences round-tripped the raw string byte-identically and would silently break under typing: the sleep generation CAS and the managed-launch CAS. Both now compare through `date_trunc('milliseconds', column)`. Verified definitively before implementation (adversarial plan review, live probes on V8, JSC, PGlite): JS `Date` parsing **truncates sub-ms lexically, never rounds** — so ms==ms is exact. That invariant is stated in a comment on both fences.

Deliberate residual, documented in code: the five raw `UPDATE … RETURNING *` reads on the warm-claim handoff paths stay raw — the audit traced every consumer and none reads a date field; converting their CASE-heavy SQL to builders is follow-up, not a bundle.

## Tests

`204 pass / 0 fail` across seven suites. New PGlite regressions (raw PGlite rows are strings exactly like production, so the harness is faithful):
- **sleep completes** — fails on base with the exact production error (`getTime is not a function`);
- **sleep CAS survives a µs-precision `updated_at`** — seeded via an explicit SQL literal because PGlite's own `NOW()` is ms-only (a NOW()-seeded row cannot reproduce the mismatch, and the fail-on-base property would be false);
- **the read returns real `Date`s and the warm-claim gate answers `true`** for a ready claimed row — pinning the upgrade-flip mechanically.

Fail-on-base verified: `0 pass / 3 fail` with `getTime is not a function` ×2. One test-only seam change: the warm-claim push suite's fake tx only speaks raw `execute()` SQL, so it now serves the lifecycle read at the helper seam (the same spy every other suite already uses), returning the same mutable row its fake tx keeps updating.

44:T1681,Closes #17363.

Follow-up repair to merged PR #17330. The merged happy path routed a simple cloud inventory ask, but it also captured lifecycle and compound turns and allowed handled failures to be delivered twice.

## What changed

- Restricts the direct heuristic to explicit, single-clause Eliza Cloud inventory asks.
- Leaves launch, delete, deploy, create, update, settings, and compound/multi-tool turns with the full planner.
- Prevents cloud-qualified inventory from falling back to the local-device `APP` action.
- Resolves the cloud action through canonical/simile metadata and removes generic local-app aliases from `LIST_CLOUD_APPS`.
- Makes populated, empty, no-key, and API-error results canonical, complete, and verified across callback and no-callback transports.
- Lets one complete verified handled failure terminal-gate while preserving `success: false`.
- Keeps queued/multiple tool calls on the evaluator path so no requested work is truncated.
- Keeps local `APP` inventory machine-readable and callback-silent.
- Adds public message-path coverage through the package-root core runtime, real Stage 1/planner/action/evaluator gate, and real Cloud action with only the SDK boundary substituted.

## Adversarial cases covered

- `launch my cloud app`
- `delete my cloud app`
- `open/show cloud app settings`
- `create and deploy a cloud app`
- `list my cloud apps and delete one`
- `list my cloud apps and launch one`
- `list my cloud apps and tell me the weather`
- comma/semicolon compound clauses
- cloud action unavailable (no local fallback)
- canonical action renamed but reachable by a cloud simile
- populated/empty/error × callback/no-callback
- sole handled failure versus a failed action with a queued second tool

46:Tf7b,Fixes #16268

Replaces closed composite PR #16269 with only the package-launcher verification contract. Store screenshot and unrelated packaging metadata changes are deliberately excluded.

## Problem

Five Snap, Debian, and Flatpak smoke steps converted failed `--version` or `--help` commands into green diagnostics. A package with a missing runtime dependency, wrong version, empty help, or a hanging launcher could therefore proceed to publication.

## Change

- Add one bounded process verifier for exact version plus populated Commander help.
- Use it in all Snap, Debian, and Flatpak smoke steps before publication or artifact attachment.
- Hard-kill commands that exceed the timeout and cap captured output.
- Cover startup failure, dependency failure, version mismatch, malformed help, hangs, wrapper arguments, CLI exit behavior, workflow masking, and publication order with real Node child processes and parsed workflow YAML.

## Exact-head verification

- 10 tests, 50 assertions.
- Changed-source line coverage: 94.64%.
- Biome, actionlint, and Node syntax check: passed.
- Error-policy, script, test-integrity, and test-realness audits: passed.
- `git diff --check`: passed.

The verifier genuinely ran `elizaos-app --version` on both installed snaps and failed closed on `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for `zod` imported by `packages/shared/src/config/config-catalog.ts`: [amd64 transcript](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29467460045/job/87523533197#step:10:1), [arm64 transcript](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29467460045/job/87523533203#step:10:1). ## What changed

- runs every `Quality (Fork)` lane on `ubuntu-24.04`, removing persistent workspace and runner-service state from untrusted fork validation
- pins the fork lane to the canonical released CI Bun version (`1.3.14`)
- isolates setup-bun's Unix `HOME` and Windows `USERPROFILE` by run, attempt, job, and matrix entry in the shared workspace action, cloud setup action, and direct CLI lane
- disables setup-bun's executable cache for those ephemeral paths, preventing per-run cache-version churn while preserving package and Turbo caches
- separates manual exact-head dispatch concurrency from pull-request metadata runs so skipped PR jobs cannot cancel queued proof
- enforces hosted selection for every current and future fork job, derives the Bun pin from `.github/ci-bun-version.json`, binds isolated homes to parsed setup-bun steps, and rejects same-repository pull requests through mutation tests

## Why

Quality (Fork) run 30688698836 assigned Build and CLI smoke to two registrations on the same Hetzner host. Both were canceled inside actions/checkout while `git clean -ffdx` was running; no setup or repository command executed. GitHub's recorded job completion timestamps precede the checkout logs, and the same registrations have produced prior canceled jobs after successful commands. Hosted runners provide a fresh workspace and process boundary for this security-sensitive fork lane.

## What was wrong

One physical model request was counted twice in database trajectories:

1. the provider-level `recordLlmCall()` emitted detailed wire telemetry;
2. `AgentRuntime.useModel()` then emitted a second generic fallback record for the same dispatch.

That exactly doubled LLM call counts, prompt/completion tokens, and cost. A global removal of either recorder would also be wrong: detailed provider telemetry should win when present, while handlers without a wire recorder still require the runtime fallback.

The audit also found a separate hole in plugin-openai's buffered streaming path. It emitted its trajectory record before filling response, tools, finish reason, usage, and cache fields, so the supposedly detailed record was incomplete.

## Deep fix

- each `AgentRuntime.useModel()` dispatch now runs in a cloned request-local trajectory context;
- a provider record marks only that dispatch after the trajectory logger actually accepts it;
- the runtime generic recorder skips only when that same dispatch already has a provider record;
- concurrent calls inherit separate AsyncLocal contexts and cannot suppress one another;
- rejected record attempts and failed provider attempts leave the generic fallback enabled;
- OpenAI buffered streams populate complete response/tool/finish/usage data before `recordLlmCall()` emits;
- OpenAI provider records now carry normalized cache-read and cache-creation token counts as well as prompt/completion counts.

No timeout, threshold, ratchet, or CI policy was added.

## Live Cerebras proof

Temporary integration build: this branch on top of #17531's owner-controlled benchmark lifecycle. Real Cerebras `gemma-4-31b`, stable cached prefix:

- exact response: `BENCHMARK-OWNER-ALLOWED-FINAL`
- benchmark response: **1,222 ms**
- physical model requests: **1**
- native trajectory: `finished`, **4,238 prompt / 53 completion / 3,328 cache-read tokens**
- DB aggregate: `completed`, `llm_call_count=1`, **4,238 / 53 / 3,328**—exactly matches native telemetry
- providers: **13 successful**, executed with overlap metadata; slowest was FACTS at 30.21 ms
- process wall including persisted-state boot and clean shutdown: **3.37 s**

- [x] [pr-17532-live-evidence.json](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-6/pr-17532-live-evidence.json)
49:T1354,## Problem

Three live incidents where a failed tool fed the planner's generic apology ("I tried to complete that, but the available runtime step failed…") instead of a real answer:

1. "can you delete all the apps on the server" — the planner staged an APP list read first; the installed-apps loopback read has a **2s deadline** that a cold registry filesystem scan legitimately exceeds, so the read threw `TimeoutError` on every cold call and the user got the apology instead of a refusal.
2. "what are your app-build routing rules?" — routed to `RUNTIME` self_status, which **hard-failed** ("Self-awareness registry is not available.") whenever the optional awareness registry isn't loaded — even though the live runtime status snapshot was available as a real answer.
3. The same 2s deadline also failed the `available_apps` provider refresh on cold boots (`[availableAppsProvider.refresh] The operation timed out.` in production logs).

## Fix

- `LOOPBACK_READ_DEADLINE_MS` 2s → 10s (read constant only; stop/launch deadlines untouched; the provider is stale-while-revalidate so composeState latency is unaffected). `runList` translates thrown transport errors (timeout / unreachable) into a **typed `success:false` result carrying tool-owned `userFacingText`** — the field the planner's grounded-failure path reads — so the user hears "Couldn't read the app list — the local app registry didn't answer in time. Try again in a moment." in voice. Caller aborts (turn cancellation) and malformed payloads still throw (pinned by test).
- `APP` gains a delete-verb class (`delete|uninstall|remove|wipe|erase|purge` — structural intent mapping like the existing LIST/STOP verb classes, checked only when no mode was inferred, so it can never shadow stop routing or explicit planner actions) returning a designed refusal that points per-app deletion at the confirmed `VIEWS action=delete` path and states bulk delete is unsupported.
- `RUNTIME` self_status degrades to the live status snapshot when the registry is absent (`error-policy:J4` designed degrade), prefixed so the caller knows the enrichment is missing.

## Testing

- New/extended tests are red on old behavior: the deadline test pinned `2_000`; runList previously propagated a bare TimeoutError (no `userFacingText` → generic fallback); a delete ask previously got the generic mode-clarify.
- app-control focused + neighbor suites 99 green; agent actions **163** green; typecheck clean in both packages.
- **Live re-probes post-fix (production Discord bot)**: delete-all → in-voice honest error; routing-rules question → real prose answer, no apology.

4b:Tcb6,Fixes #17489. Follow-up to merged #17481.

## Root cause

The merged implementation applied an implicit 3-second deadline to every provider, raced without aborting provider work, and cached a timeout as empty context. That is unsafe for correctness-critical history and for roughly 331 provider declarations whose latency semantics were never audited. The cited `available_apps` 10.9-second root path was already repaired separately by its stale-while-revalidate snapshot.

## Repair

- Deadlines are opt-in per provider; `ELIZA_COMPOSE_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` remains an explicit operator-wide override.
- Every execution receives a derived signal combining parent-turn cancellation and its deadline.
- Timeout fails composition by default, preserving all-or-nothing state and preventing partial cache writes.
- Only reviewed supplemental providers use `timeoutMode: "degrade"`; their prompt and structured state explicitly say unavailable/deadline_exceeded.
- Conversation history, facts, and relationships receive longer explicit budgets and remain fail-fast.
- Coalesced callers share one deadline. Late non-cooperative reads cannot replace the cached timeout result.
- Provider-owned errors named `TimeoutError` are no longer misclassified as runtime deadlines.
- Removes the unsupported default 500 ms post-reply WARN added by #17481; no production baseline justified that global log threshold.

Exact tested base: `72d985d2fca036875308acb3eae45e1cf2c5f285`
Exact head: `1f4582f58991de27be13a71d2a030adb4c1ce9bb`

## Verification

- Core focused compose budget/execution/purity/inference: 4 files, 31/31 passed.
- Core affected providers and DB I/O concurrency: 4 files, 63/63 passed.
- Agent recent/relevant conversation providers: 3 files, 8/8 passed.
- `bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck`: passed.
- `bun run --cwd packages/agent typecheck`: passed.
- `bun run --cwd packages/core build`: passed (Node/browser/edge/testing/declarations).
- `bun run --cwd packages/agent build`: passed.
- Biome check on every changed file and `git diff --check`: passed.

The deterministic benchmark proves a 300 ms optional deadline returns before 1.5-second work and a 300 ms fail deadline returns before 1.5-second work. The production trajectory claimed in #17481 was not attached and is not reproducible from this checkout, so it is not presented as evidence here.

# Background

  addition to `[data-view-status="loading"]`.
- Polls within the existing bounded readiness window and records a render-state
  failure when loading does not settle.
- Preserves hidden, display-none, and offscreen stale-marker behavior.
- Adds a first-party finance OCR contract that requires a ready, empty, or
  designed-error label and independently forbids terminal `Loading`.
- Adds focused regressions for loaded, persistent-loading, settled, hidden, and
  strict-locator states plus finance OCR ready/empty/error/loading states.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"anthropic","model":"claude-mycode","client":"Claude Agent SDK","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@4b0bad53a7fd5cc0c1f0c507c849f27ebb53b1d3:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->4f:Tbe8,Reopens #17494 (closed by @lalalune) as a new PR pinned to the repaired `0.3.0` releases, per their explicit request.

## The blocking finding

All four clients (`dist/index.js:44-78` in the published `0.2.0` artifacts) decoded the untrusted x402 challenge from the remote server and passed its `amount`/`payTo`/`asset`/`network` straight into `createPaymentHeader` with no local check. A malicious or compromised server (or anyone who took over one of the DuckDNS endpoints) could request an arbitrarily large USDC authorization and the agent would sign it automatically. The reviewer is correct — this is a real signing flaw, not a supply-chain nitpick, and the prior happy-path mainnet transactions did not bound it.

## The fix (published as `0.3.0` on npm for all four packages)

Each plugin's `src/client.ts` now runs `assertChallengeWithinPolicy()` on the raw, untrusted `accepts` option **before** `createPaymentHeader` is ever called:

- Exact match required on `scheme` (`"exact"`), `network` (`eip155:8453`, Base mainnet — the only chain these clients ever sign for, even though ChainScope's server also offers a Solana leg), and `asset` (the real Base USDC contract address).
- Exact match required on `payTo` against the plugin's known seller wallet.
- A per-request maximum amount (`$0.25`–`$1.00` depending on the plugin's real price ceiling, generous headroom over the advertised prices but a real backstop), configurable via a `*_MAX_PAYMENT_USD` runtime setting if a legitimate price rises later.
- Any mismatch throws (`"Refusing to pay: ..."`) before signing — no exception path reaches `createPaymentHeader`.

## Adversarial tests

Each plugin gained a `src/__tests__/paymentPolicy.test.ts` with 7 cases: a legitimate-challenge baseline, then one test each tampering with amount (above ceiling), `payTo`, `asset`, `network`, and `scheme` — each asserting `createPaymentHeader` was **never called** and the action fails closed with a `"Refusing to pay"` error — plus one test confirming a raised ceiling via the runtime setting still works. 55/55 tests pass across the four packages (up from 27 before this fix), `tsc --noEmit` is clean, and the compiled `dist/index.js` for all four packages was grepped post-build to confirm the policy code is actually present in the shipped artifact, not just the source.

## What this PR does not claim to fix

Package provenance (`npm publish --provenance` needs CI, not set up) and DuckDNS-hosted domains remain as they were — the reviewer correctly separated those from this signing flaw, and they're still open follow-ups, not hidden.

All four confirmed live on the npm registry at `0.3.0` as of this PR.
50:Ta90,Re-submission after review on the previous PR (closed, same branch) found a real bug: the paid action could not actually complete a payment against the registered client. Root cause and fix, in full:

**Root cause (3 stacked wire-format mismatches between x402-fetch@1.2.0 and our server's actual V2 contract):**
1. Our server sends the 402 challenge only in the `Payment-Required` **header** (empty JSON body) -- `x402-fetch`'s `wrapFetchWithPayment()` only reads the body.
2. Our server's `network` field is CAIP-2 (`eip155:8453`); the JS client matches against short names (`base`).
3. The actual current V2 `PaymentPayload` schema is `{x402Version, payload, accepted: <full requirements object>}` -- not the `{x402Version, scheme, network, payload}` shape `x402-fetch`'s client produces. `find_matching_requirements()` reads `payload.accepted.*` server-side *before* ever contacting the facilitator.

**Fix:** each plugin now builds the request/pay/retry flow directly on `x402/client`'s lower-level primitives (`createPaymentHeader`, `selectPaymentRequirements`) instead of the higher-level wrapper, and assembles the correct V2 wire payload by hand. Verified by reading the actual Python x402 SDK source (`x402/schemas/payments.py`, `x402/server_base.py`) against the JS client source, not by guessing.

**Real, complete, funded end-to-end proof for all 4 (not just the one endpoint requested) -- real wallet, real signed payment, real settlement, real data, real Base mainnet transactions:**

| Plugin | Price | Transaction |
|---|---|---|
| plugin-concordance | $0.01 | `0x2d8d014d369647cf1bc2af2850c085e37531f5015dd1bf32ae9724ddd0049af9` |
| plugin-trading-strategy-data | $0.04 | settled (verified via balance delta) |
| plugin-trustfetch | $0.03 | `0x0ad45a40b9b0302d076a472bc90e511c934133f7fd6a1b339f0f1586412b67fe` |
| plugin-chainscope | $0.03 | `0x67e9ce78bde5b018da842afbf2e7bfd3a60f7d32593359e71e57f3f834814e80` |

All 4 verifiable on Base mainnet (network `eip155:8453`).

**On the supply-chain concerns raised:** all 4 repos/npm packages were young at first submission -- they're now a few days older, with real commit history from this fix. `npm publish --provenance` genuinely requires a supported CI OIDC identity (GitHub Actions/GitLab) and cannot be produced by a local `npm publish`; happy to wire up a publish workflow as a follow-up if that's wanted, but did not want to block this fix on it. The four domains remain DuckDNS subdomains -- a real, known limitation, disclosed rather than hidden.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds a `plugin-tests` job to `.github/workflows/develop-pr.yml` so plugin unit
tests run **pre-merge** for PRs targeting `develop`.

The job resolves which plugin packages the PR actually changed (from the PR base
SHA), builds a `(plugins/<pkg>)`-anchored regex, and runs those packages' own
`test` scripts through `packages/scripts/run-all-tests.mjs` — the same runner and
the same `TEST_PACKAGE_FILTER` / `TEST_SCRIPT_FILTER` surface the post-merge
`test:plugins` lane already uses. `--min-tasks=1` makes the gate fail closed
rather than report green when the filter selects nothing.

## What kind of change is this?

CI configuration. No product code, no runtime behavior, no rendered surface.

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

Per #17550: `test.yml` has no `pull_request` trigger and `windows-ci.yml`'s PR
trigger is scoped to `main`, so a defect only a plugin spec can catch reaches
`develop` and is found post-merge. Scoped deliberately to the develop PR gate to
avoid conflicting with #17529, which is editing `windows-ci.yml`'s trigger. No
change to post-merge coverage in `test.yml`; no duplication of the Windows lane.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The job carries inline comments explaining the scoping and the timeout.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The `plugin-tests` job in `.github/workflows/develop-pr.yml`, then the red/green
evidence below.

## Detailed testing steps

The acceptance criterion asks for a demonstrated red/green pair on
`plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/app.test.ts:43`. On current `develop`
that assertion is **already red** — #17539 ("test(app-control): align owner
denial copy") is still open, so the tree still asserts `"only the owner"` while
`app.ts:325` ships `"Sorry — only my owner can manage apps."`.

That is the issue's own thesis, confirmed: a plugin-spec regression is sitting on
`develop` right now precisely because nothing runs these specs pre-merge.

RED — gate command on unmodified `develop`:

```
TEST_PACKAGE_FILTER='\(plugins/(plugin-app-control)\)' TEST_SCRIPT_FILTER='^test$' \
  node packages/scripts/run-all-tests.mjs --only=test --no-cloud --concurrency=3 --min-tasks=1

 Test Files  4 failed | 43 passed (47)
      Tests  1 failed | 1600 passed (1601)

AssertionError: expected { success: false, …(1) } to deeply equal ObjectContaining{…}
-   "text": StringContaining "only the owner"
+   "text": "Sorry — only my owner can manage apps."
 ❯ src/actions/app.test.ts:40:18

[eliza-test] FAIL @elizaos/plugin-app-control (plugins/plugin-app-control)#test
```

GREEN — same command with #17539's one-line assertion change applied locally
(applied only to demonstrate; **not** included in this PR, since that line
belongs to #17539):

```
 Test Files  3 failed | 44 passed (47)
      Tests  1601 passed (1601)
```

The targeted spec goes 1-failed → 0-failed and the suite reaches 1601/1601
passing, which is the behavior the gate exists to catch.

# Known limitations a reviewer must weigh

1. **Two `plugin-app-control` view specs cannot load on my runner.** In both the
   red and green runs, `src/views/ViewManagerView.render.test.tsx`,
   `src/views/viewManagerData.contract.test.ts` and

   package declares `main: ./dist/plugin.cjs.js` and has no `dist/` after
   `bun run build:core`. So the gate command exits non-zero on my host even when
   every test passes (1601/1601). Whether a hosted `ubuntu-latest` runner
   reproduces this is **unverified** — this PR's own `plugin-tests` run is the
   cheapest way to settle it. If CI reproduces it, this job needs either a build
   step that produces that package's `dist/`, or agreement to scope the gate
   away from view specs, before it is made a required check.
2. **Do not mark this job required until #17539 lands.** `plugin-app-control`'s
   spec is red on `develop` today, so a required gate would block every PR
   touching that package. Required-check selection is a branch-protection
   setting, not part of this diff.
3. Added wall-clock: on this host the gated command takes ~15-19s for one plugin
   package on top of ~1m21s for `build:core`. The job is skipped entirely when a
   PR changes no plugin package with a `test` script.

# Risks

Low. This is a test-only change in one Playwright UI-smoke spec. It adds no production runtime, API, schema, persistence, or dependency changes. The main risk is future fixture drift in the real-bundle test path.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds a focused 844×390 Notes regression that keeps the real chat overlay visible, scrolls through the root Notes flow, selects the green composer color, saves a note, and proves both controls are pointer-reachable. It also asserts the composer remains `position: static`, the root actually scrolls, the `create-note` request payload is correct, the response is 200, the created note renders, and browser diagnostics stay clean.

The production repair is already present on `develop`; this PR supplies the missing interaction regression and reproducible evidence. A red control temporarily restored the historical sticky composer locally and failed on pointer reachability; that temporary production edit was reverted and is not part of this diff.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug-fix regression coverage (non-breaking, test-only).

My changes do not require a project documentation update.

stack swipe, and explicit collapse at desktop and mobile viewports.

# Risks

**Medium** — changes rested home flex allocation for all viewports (not LP3-only). Expand/collapse settle transitions and secondary-content displacement still use the existing `:has()` rules. Mitigated by unit coverage of the CSS contract + expand/collapse focus tests, and by keeping the secondary min band so widgets cannot go to zero while the shade is rested.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes the LP3 short-panel home regression where rested notifications were hard-clamped to `max-height: 40%` of the content column (~366px on 1080×1240), leaving roughly two cards visible.

**Change:** replace the rested clamp with a content-first allocation:

```css
[data-testid="home-content-column"] {
  --eliza-home-secondary-min: 8.5rem;
}
[data-home-notification-region] {
  max-height: calc(100% - var(--eliza-home-secondary-min, 8.5rem));
}
[data-home-below-notifications] {
  min-height: var(--eliza-home-secondary-min, 8.5rem);
}
```

When the shade expands, secondary content still collapses (`min-height: 0` + `grid-template-rows: 0fr`) via the existing `:has()` rules.

**Explicit non-goal:** do **not** re-embed the app launcher dock on the home page. Home↔Apps remains a horizontal pager (`HomeLauncherSurface`); unit and e2e contracts require apps exclusively on the adjacent launcher page. Zero-area Settings/Wallet nodes **while on the home half** are off-page rail content under that architecture, not a second layout bug fixed here.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

Issue #17502 measured the regression between demo-day `d2dfd159` and later `develop` on physical LP3. Root cause matches commit `282c82c` / PR #17151 introducing `max-height: 40%` on `[data-home-notification-region]`. On short portrait free space that is a ~46% height loss vs demo-day (~678px → ~366px).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Risks

**Low.** Test-harness + small production collaborator indirection in Signal setup
routes only. Pairing/auth still defaults to the real `pairing-service` bindings;
tests swap that surface without reloading the route module. Process-local session
maps still need isolation between cases (explicit test reset).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

#17402 moved the cold Signal setup-route import into `beforeAll`, but each case
still paid `vi.resetModules()` + dynamic re-import. That keeps isolation at the
cost of repeated module execution and encourages oversized hook timeouts.

This change:

1. Imports `setup-routes` once (static import).
2. Routes pairing/auth through injectable collaborators (production defaults to
   real `pairing-service` exports).
3. Resets process-local pairing session/snapshot maps between cases.
4. Adds a contract test that the route module evaluation count stays `1` across
   warm cases and that warm handlers stay under a tight duration budget.
5. Removes the 120s cold-import hook timeout masking.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes / test-harness improvement (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `plugins/plugin-signal/src/setup-routes.ts` — collaborator binding + test resets
2. `plugins/plugin-signal/src/setup-routes.test.ts` — single import + injected fakes
3. Focused + full plugin test output below

## Detailed testing steps

## Summary

- exclude GitHub Actions runner-owned control directories from workspace prune plans
- keep stale ordinary repository workspaces eligible for deletion
- add regression coverage for every protected directory

The pruner previously treated every direct child of `_work` as a repository checkout. On self-hosted runners that includes `_temp`, where Actions keeps live `save_state_*` command files, plus action and tool caches. Runs 30692175805 and 30691649390 both failed after those files disappeared on `eliza-robot-6`.

5a:Ta0a,Fixes #17495.

## What changed

- Replaced all three lease-heartbeat tests' fixed-duration scheduler assumptions with event-driven gates.
- The active-renewal case waits for the observed second renewal; settlement/loss cases hold both execution and renewal deterministically, then release them in order.
- Every in-flight `processPendingJobs` promise is awaited in `finally`, including assertion/timeout failures, so intervals and rejected work cannot leak into later tests.
- The observation deadline is bounded at 500 ms (25× the configured 20 ms interval): missing heartbeats fail without a hang while preserving useful interval sensitivity.
- Added `develop` to the Windows workflow's pull-request branches and a contract test, so Windows fixes are proven before merge rather than only on the post-merge push.
- Removed the unrelated app-control copy assertion from this PR; that independent stale assertion is isolated in #17525.

## Root cause

Windows run 30669553618 exposed tests that inferred heartbeat count from how many `setInterval` callbacks happened during a fixed 90 ms job. That is not a portable scheduler contract. The tests now synchronize on the repository callback itself while keeping the job pending, so they assert lease behavior rather than wall-clock luck.

5b:Td25,## Problem

Live production incident from a 30-scenario battery: "whats todays date and time?" was answered **"sunday, july 20, 2026. 10:42 am."** — 11 days wrong, wrong weekday — while the runtime's system prompt explicitly promises a time signal is always present.

Root cause: Stage-1 excluded `CURRENT_TIME` behind a prose-regex that guessed whether the user's message was a time question (`isCurrentTimeQuestion`). Any phrasing the regex missed dropped the clock from context, and the model hallucinated a date with full confidence.

## Fix

The intent-guess regex and the `CURRENT_TIME` entry in `STAGE1_EXTRA_PROVIDER_EXCLUSIONS` are deleted. The time provider is pure synchronous formatting (sub-millisecond, ~60 tokens), so it now renders on **every** Stage-1/simple-path turn — matching the system-prompt contract and the planner path, which already always included it. The expensive `ENTITIES`/`DOCUMENTS` exclusions are retained; the provider budget/degrade machinery is untouched.

## Testing

- New tests pin the incident: the exact live phrasing (and a no-time-intent message) must render `# Current Time` into the model messages — **red on pre-fix code** (the removed regex rejects both phrasings).
- `stage1-provider-execution` 4/4, `message-runtime-stage1` 102/102, budget/purity/refresh regression suites 32/32, tsc clean.
- **Live re-probe post-fix on a production Discord bot**: same question → "saturday, august 1, 2026. 02:06 utc." — correct date, weekday, and time.

5c:T460,## Problem
The self-hosted runner workspace pruner (`packages/scripts/cloud/admin/prune-runner-workspaces.ts`) treats every direct child of `_work` as a disposable repository checkout. GitHub Actions also owns persistent control directories there, including `_temp`, `_actions`, `_tool`, `_update`, and `_PipelineMapping`.

This deletes `_work/_temp/_runner_file_commands/save_state_*` while steps run, causing unrelated workflow steps to fail after checkout.

## Solution
- Defined `PROTECTED_RUNNER_CONTROL_DIRS` containing `_temp`, `_actions`, `_tool`, `_update`, and `_PipelineMapping` in `packages/scripts/cloud/admin/prune-runner-workspaces.ts`.
- Updated `buildRunnerWorkspacePrunePlan` to skip runner control directories when discovering prune targets under `_work`.
- Added unit test coverage in `packages/scripts/cloud/admin/prune-runner-workspaces.test.ts` verifying all protected control directories are preserved while stale repository checkouts remain eligible.

Closes #17534

- verifies importing the module stays side-effect free under Node and Bun
- preserves any pre-existing nonzero process exit state instead of assigning `process.exitCode = 0`

This avoids platform-specific drive-letter, slash, URL-encoding, casing, and symlink behavior entirely.

# Risks

Low. The change renames one output field (`effectiveParallelism` → `providerSpanOverlap`) and one local variable in the operator benchmark CLI `packages/core/scripts/provider-latency-report.ts`, plus its process-contract test. Repo-wide search shows no other consumer of the field name. No runtime, provider, or authorization behavior changes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Completes the last required repair item of #17443: "Rename or redefine `effectiveParallelism`: the current metric is provider-span overlap and does not prove concurrent database execution."

The metric divides summed provider span time by composition wall time. Overlapping spans do not prove concurrently executing database reads — adapter calls can still serialize under overlapping spans — so the old name overclaimed what the benchmark measures. The field is now `providerSpanOverlap`, the sample variable is `spanOverlapSamples`, the file header says "provider-span overlap" instead of "observed concurrency", and a comment at the computation states exactly what the ratio does and does not prove.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvements (metric naming accuracy in an operator benchmark; non-breaking — the report JSON is written to an operator-chosen path and has no in-repo consumer of the renamed field).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The report field is documented by the script and its test, both updated here.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/core/scripts/provider-latency-report.test.ts` — the process-contract test spawns the real CLI against the real PGLite-backed runtime and asserts the report shape, including the renamed `providerSpanOverlap` distribution.

bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck

63:T73f,# Relates to

Windows CI regression: importing the command-coverage contract under Bun could be misclassified as direct execution because a hand-built `file://` URL did not match Windows drive-letter paths.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Targets `develop` and includes current `develop` at exact head `7c186c1bc18937601c8f5ebd96e6e414837ec0c7`.
- [x] Merge state is clean.

# Risks

Low. One CLI entrypoint guard changes from manual URL concatenation to Node’s platform-aware `pathToFileURL(resolve(...))`; exported contract behavior is unchanged.

# Background

On Windows, `import.meta.url` is a normalized `file:///C:/...` URL while ``file://${process.argv[1]}`` contains backslashes and the wrong slash count. The old guard therefore ran the CLI body during test imports. The replacement uses Node’s URL conversion and handles a missing argv entry.

# Testing

- `bun test --config=/dev/null packages/scripts/__tests__/ci-windows-command-coverage-contract.test.ts` — 6/6 pass.
- Direct Node and Bun invocations produce the expected success line.
- The Windows CI lane is the platform proof for drive-letter semantics.

Closes #17495

## Problem
Windows CI run 30669553618 failed on current develop in two test assertions:
1. `provisioning-jobs-lease-heartbeat.test.ts` assumed a fixed 90 ms job execution window was sufficient to observe >= 2 heartbeat interval callbacks (20 ms interval). Under Windows runner scheduler load/contention, callbacks can coalesce, causing only 1 callback to fire before the hardcoded timer completes.
2. `plugin-app-control/src/actions/app.test.ts` asserted `text: expect.stringContaining("only the owner")`, whereas the actual shipped owner-gate denial message in `app.ts` is `"Sorry — only my owner can manage apps."`.

## Fix
1. In `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/provisioning-jobs-lease-heartbeat.test.ts`, replaced the hardcoded 90 ms sleep with a `Promise` latch + `pollUntil` helper that keeps the job execution active until at least 2 renewals have been observed (with a 2,000 ms timeout deadline). This makes the renewal assertions completely platform-timer-agnostic.
2. In `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/app.test.ts`, updated the string assertion to `expect.stringContaining("only my owner")` to match the shipped denial response.

## Evidence
- `git diff` clean and verified against `develop` head.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/app.test.ts` string assertion matches `app.ts` line 325.
- `provisioning-jobs-lease-heartbeat.test.ts` execution latch satisfies the issue's acceptance criteria: "Keep the lease execution active until the test has observed the required renewal count".

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Deletes three disconnected simulation layers:

- an ordinary `.test.ts` file that silently made paid Cerebras/Anthropic calls whenever ambient credentials existed;
- a hardcoded responder whose invented output was graded by an LLM and presented as agent behavior;
- an unused catalog that generated 340 prose variants behind an exact 10x ratchet, plus a source-parsing mock planner that never invoked the shipped VIEWS action.

Real view behavior remains covered by `plugin-app-control`, agent registry/HTTP/WebSocket tests, scenario-runner active-view scenarios, and app browser flows. There is no replacement judge because judging fabricated output is not evidence.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and test simplification.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The deleted catalog was test-only and had no production or documentation consumer.
